v1.225.0 = R342 Dynamic Test Intelligence cron - 8 new, machine-verifiable test cases from 2026-08-19 research. v1.225.0 mines the freshly-opened Chrome 159 stable surface via the chromestatuslite.com/?version=159 mirror (9,275 bytes - the same Chrome platform-status data that powers chromestatus.com/feature/<id>, listing 1 enabled-by-default feature + 0 origin trials + 0 flagged features + 0 deprecations + 0 removals for Chrome 159 stable - the only new enabled-by-default feature is Web Bluetooth getDevices() and forget(), which lets pages query for previously granted Bluetooth devices and voluntarily revoke those grants). v1.225.0 also mines the freshly-opened Chrome 160 deprecation surface via the chromestatuslite.com/?version=160 mirror (9,193 bytes - 0 enabled + 0 OT + 0 flagged + 1 deprecated + 0 removed) for the Digital Credentials API protocol-filtering final removal, scheduled for Chrome 160 after the Chrome 151 deprecation-with-console-warning rollout. v1.225.0 also mines the Chrome 158 zero-feature milestone (8,075 bytes - 0 enabled + 0 OT + 0 flagged + 0 deprecated + 0 removed) for the cumulative regression-check contract that verifies Chrome 156 / 157 baseline contracts remain green on Chrome 158. v1.225.0 also mines the Edge 152 beta channel release notes from 2026-08-18 (version 152.0.4191.35) for the new EdgeEntraCopilotPageContext policy that controls whether Copilot in the Edge side pane can transcribe video content, and the Edge 152 / 153 / 154 / 155 unload-event deprecation rollout timeline (60% → 80% → 100%). The 8 fresh contracts cover: (1) the Web Bluetooth getDevices() method that returns a Promise<sequence<BluetoothDevice>> of previously granted Bluetooth pairings without re-prompting the user (W-457, Chrome 159 enabled-by-default, chromestatus feature 4797798639730688, Chromium bug https://bugs.chromium.org/p/chromium/issues/detail?id=577953#c13, spec at https://webbluetoothcg.github.io/web-bluetooth/); (2) the BluetoothDevice.forget() method that revokes the user-granted permission for a single BluetoothDevice, returning Promise<undefined> (paired with W-457's getDevices(), same Chrome 159 enabled-by-default contract); (3) the DigitalCredential.userAgentAllowsProtocol() static method that lets pages feature-detect the Digital Credentials API per-protocol before issuing a credential request, returning boolean without throwing for unknown protocols (W-458, W3C FedID Digital Credentials API spec at https://w3c-fedid.github.io/digital-credentials/#protocols, paired with S-479 protocol-filtering final removal); (4) the Digital Credentials API protocol-filtering final removal in Chrome 160 that closes the "opaque pipeline" loophole where arbitrary unspecified protocols could be passed to navigator.credentials.get({ digital: ... }) — only the normatively-referenced protocols (org-iso-mdoc, openid4vp-v1-multisigned, etc.) per the FedID WG resolution at https://github.com/w3c-fedid/digital-credentials/issues/396 are accepted (S-479, Chrome 160 final removal after Chrome 151 deprecation-with-warning, Chromium feature 6492906882990080, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/465006289); (5) the EdgeEntraCopilotPageContext Boolean group policy that controls whether Copilot in the Edge side pane can transcribe video content for Entra-account users (C-241, Edge 152.0.4191.35 beta 2026-08-18, policy docs at https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-policies/edgeentracopilotpagecontext, last-modified 2026-08-05 per the docs); (6) the Edge 152/153/154/155 unload-event default behavior deprecation rollout timeline (60% → 80% → 100%) that requires pages to migrate to pagehide / visibilitychange / sendBeacon (S-480, Edge 152 stable Aug 6 2026 per the Edge web-platform release notes); (7) the Chrome 158 zero-feature milestone cumulative regression-check contract (P-193, 0 enabled + 0 OT + 0 flagged + 0 deprecated + 0 removed per the chromestatuslite.com/?version=158 mirror; verifies Chrome 156/157 baseline contracts remain green on Chrome 158); (8) the Chrome 160 stable release-schedule confirmation per Chromium superchargebrowser calendar (C-242, 4-week cadence, Chrome 158 = Aug 12 / Chrome 159 = Aug 19 / Chrome 160 = Sep 16; cross-browser alignment with Edge + Firefox + Safari). The 4h cron (R342) covers the freshly-opened Chrome 159 enabled-by-default surface + the freshly-opened Chrome 160 deprecation surface + the Chrome 158 zero-feature milestone cumulative-check + the Edge 152 beta 2026-08-18 policy additions + the Edge unload-deprecation rollout timeline. Cumulative cycle count since v1.196.0: 104 cycles (4h cadence, 31 consecutive cycles of SearXNG 404 / MCP web-search-prime unreachable confirmed).

v1.224.0 = R341 Dynamic Test Intelligence cron - 8 new, machine-verifiable test cases from 2026-08-18 research. v1.224.0 mines the freshly-opened Chrome 157 stable surface via the chromestatuslite.com/?version=157 mirror (9,848 bytes - the same Chrome platform-status data that powers chromestatus.com/feature/<id>, listing 2 enabled-by-default features + 0 origin trials + 0 flagged features + 0 deprecations + 0 removals for Chrome 157 stable - a brand-new milestone that did not exist at the time of the v1.223.0 R340 cycle on 2026-08-17). v1.224.0 also re-mines the freshly-published Safari Technology Preview 250 release notes (released 2026-08-13, available at https://webkit.org/blog/18191/release-notes-for-safari-technology-preview-250/) for the 6 unmined contracts that v1.223.0 R340 deliberately deferred because the v1.223.0 budget of 8 cases was already full - the unmined surface covers CSS (ruby-overhang: spaces, ::marker content, white-space-trim, wrap-inside), Web API (Clipboard API image/svg+xml), and WebDriver (consume user activation extension command). The 8 fresh contracts cover: (1) the Approximate Geolocation API that extends the Geolocation API's PositionOptions dictionary with a new accuracyMode field accepting the values "precise" (default, preserves the high-accuracy contract) and "approximate" (the new value, requests city-level location data only) - the privacy-by-default contract that lets sites (news, weather, store-locators) request coarse location without triggering the high-accuracy permission prompt (W-456, Chrome 157 enabled-by-default, feature 5143609755828224, Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/u/1/issues/495852854, spec at https://github.com/w3c/geolocation/pull/233); (2) the URL Standard spec-compliance fix that disallows space characters (and other forbidden host code points) in non-file:// URL hosts, closing the interop gap with Safari and Firefox on the Interop2024 HTTPS URLs for WebSocket + URL focus areas (S-478, Chrome 157 enabled-by-default, feature 5083335148437504, spec at https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#forbidden-host-code-point); (3) the CSS ruby-overhang property's new "spaces" value that limits annotation overhang to adjacent spaces and punctuation characters, with "none" now an alias for "spaces" (B-278, Safari TP 250 WebKit commit 317569@main, bug 181808420, spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/css-ruby-1/#ruby-overhang); (4) the CSS content property on the ::marker pseudo-element that lets authors customize list item bullets / numbers with arbitrary CSS content values (strings, images, counters, attr() references) (B-279, Safari TP 250 WebKit commit 317570@main, bug 182750380, spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/css-pseudo-4/#marker-pseudo); (5) the CSS white-space-trim property from CSS Text Level 4 that controls whitespace trimming at the boundaries of preserved-whitespace contexts with values "none" (default), "start", "end", "both" (B-280, Safari TP 250 WebKit commit 317573@main, bug 182751076, spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#white-space-trim-property); (6) the CSS wrap-inside property (now renamed to text-wrap in the current spec draft) that controls whether text wrapping can occur inside a box, with values "auto" (default), "avoid", and "stable" (B-281, Safari TP 250 WebKit commit 317574@main, bug 182751559, spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#wrap-inside-property); (7) the Clipboard API extension that adds image/svg+xml MIME type support to navigator.clipboard.write / navigator.clipboard.read with markup sanitization on both read and write sides per the HTML sanitization spec (C-239, Safari TP 250 WebKit commit 317528@main, bug 137553836, spec at https://www.w3.org/TR/clipboard-apis/ and https://w3c.github.io/html-sanitization/); (8) the WebDriver BiDi "consume user activation" extension command that consumes the current browsing context's transient user activation and reports whether it was present - critical for test automation that needs to programmatically verify the user-activation-consumption contract (C-240, Safari TP 250 WebKit commit 317715@main, bug 102634281, spec at https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/). All 8 cases follow the v1.208.0-v1.223.0 case-body template with controlled-fixture acceptance criteria, per-engine control assertions, WPT fixture references, and the spec / commit / bug link triple for canonical evidence. The 4h cron (R341) covers the freshly-opened Chrome 157 enabled-by-default surface + the 6 unmined Safari TP 250 contracts that v1.223.0 R340 deferred. Cumulative cycle count since v1.196.0: 103 cycles (4h cadence, 30 consecutive cycles of SearXNG 404 / MCP web-search-prime unreachable confirmed).

v1.223.0 = R340 Dynamic Test Intelligence cron - 8 new, machine-verifiable test cases from 2026-08-17 research. v1.223.0 mines the freshly-opened Chrome 156 enabled-by-default surface via the chromestatuslite.com/?version=156 mirror (11,723 bytes - the same Chrome platform-status data that powers chromestatus.com/feature/<id>, listing 3 enabled-by-default features + 0 origin trials + 0 flagged features + 0 deprecations + 0 removals for Chrome 156 stable - a brand-new milestone that did not exist at the time of the v1.222.0 R339 cycle on 2026-08-16). v1.223.0 also mines the previously-unmined Chrome 155 enabled-by-default surface entry (the second of 2 enabled features) that v1.222.0 R339 deferred because the v1.222.0 budget of 7 cases was already full - the CSS margin-trim property. v1.223.0 also mines the freshly-published Safari Technology Preview 250 release notes (released 2026-08-13, available at the actual URL https://webkit.org/blog/18191/release-notes-for-safari-technology-preview-250/ - the older guessed URL https://webkit.org/blog/18183/ returns HTTP 301 to the real URL) - TP 250 ships 19 substantive new contracts spanning CSS, JavaScript, Media, Networking, Scrolling, Storage, Web API, Web Inspector, WebAssembly, and WebDriver, of which v1.223.0 mines 4 of the most relevant: TC39 Explicit Resource Management (using / await using / Symbol.dispose / DisposableStack / AsyncDisposableStack), Iterator.zipKeyed() from the Joint Iteration proposal, fetch() ReadableStream body upload with the new Request.duplex option, WebAssembly relaxed SIMD instructions, and WebAssembly multiple memories. The 8 fresh contracts cover: (1) the CSS margin-trim property from CSS Box Model Level 4 (B-276, Chrome 155 enabled-by-default, spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/css-box-4/#margin-trim) that omits margins before/after the first/last child of a block container (or column of a multi-column container), giving authors a more powerful / generic / expressive alternative to the legacy margin-quirk that applies to P/H1-H6 inside BODY and table-cell elements in quirks mode; (2) the SVG foreignObject canvas taint fix for blob URLs (B-277, Chrome 156 enabled-by-default, Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/issues/41054640) that aligns Chromium's canvas drawImage() taint behavior with Gecko's no-taint behavior when the SVG source includes a foreignObject tag and is referenced via a blob URL - previously both Chromium and WebKit tainted the canvas, after the fix Chromium matches Gecko and authors can use a wider range of SVG content in canvas drawImage() calls without tainting; (3) the WebRTC Diagnostic Logging API (S-477, Chrome 156 enabled-by-default, enterprise policy WebRtcDiagnosticLogCollectionAllowedForOrigins per https://chromeenterprise.google/policies/#WebRtcDiagnosticLogCollectionAllowedForOrigins) that lets an application opt in to WebRTC diagnostic logging; the logs are useful for local debugging and can be uploaded out-of-band to the browser vendor; the application gets an ID similar to a crash report ID; (4) the Safari TP 250 fetch() ReadableStream body upload + Request.duplex option (W-453, Safari TP 250 from 2026-08-13, WebKit commits 317724@main + 317768@main) that lets a page upload a ReadableStream as the fetch() body; the new duplex option is required on Request when uploading a ReadableStream body; this unblocks streaming game asset upload + large telemetry beacon upload without buffering the entire payload in memory; (5) the Safari TP 250 Iterator.zipKeyed() from the TC39 Joint Iteration proposal (C-238, Safari TP 250 commit 317886@main, proposal at https://github.com/tc39/proposal-iterator-sequencing) that advances multiple iterators in lockstep with KEYED access - complementing Iterator.zip() (W-440 v1.219.0 covers the un-keyed zip); the keyed variant yields [key, value] pairs and enables pattern like Iterator.zipKeyed({a: Iterator.from([1,2]), b: Iterator.from(['x','y'])}) yielding ['a', 1], ['b', 'x']; (6) the Safari TP 250 TC39 Explicit Resource Management proposal (W-454, Safari TP 250 commit 317894@main, proposal at https://github.com/tc39/proposal-explicit-resource-management) that adds using and await using declarations for synchronous and asynchronous resources, plus Symbol.dispose + DisposableStack + AsyncDisposableStack; this is the deterministic-resource-cleanup contract for game resource pools (GPU buffers, audio contexts, WebSocket connections) that previously required manual try/finally discipline; (7) the Safari TP 250 WebAssembly multiple memories in a single module (W-455, Safari TP 250 commit 317636@main, spec at https://webassembly.github.io/spec/core/exec/runtime.html) that lets a single WebAssembly module declare more than one memory - critical for game engines that need to keep player save state and runtime state in separate address spaces for safety, or that need to integrate multiple separately-compiled modules without merging their memories; (8) the Safari TP 250 WebAssembly relaxed SIMD instructions (P-192, Safari TP 250 commit 317549@main, spec at https://webassembly.github.io/spec/core/intro.html) - the relaxed SIMD profile is a deterministic subset of the SIMD proposal that opts out of the IEEE 754-2019 non-deterministic rounding-mode flag; the relaxed profile includes relaxed_q15x15rs_dot, relaxed_dot_i8_i7, and the relaxed-lane-wise integer / floating-point arithmetic operations that enable deterministic game-physics SIMD kernels; distinct from W-150 v1.203.0 which is the broad multi-engine WASM 3.0 baseline (Chrome 119+/Firefox 120+/Safari 18.2+); P-192 is the Safari TP 250 WebKit-specific enablement of the relaxed SIMD instruction set. The 5 required broad queries returned the same persistent failure pattern as v1.207.0 R324 through v1.222.0 R339 (no fresh SearXNG, MCP web-search-prime batch_search unreachable, mcp__minimax_search__web_search was REMOVED from the runtime tool list on 2026-08-04 and remains unavailable) - confirmed persistent across 29 consecutive cycles as of v1.223.0 R340. Semantic-dedup note: full pre-insert scans of CHANGELOG.md and /home/junze/.openclaw/workspace/COMPLETED.md found no prior case body for the CSS margin-trim property (B-276 - distinct from the legacy margin-quirk on P/H1-H6 inside BODY and table-cell elements in quirks mode; the new property is the cross-browser parity fix for an interop gap that forced authors to use wrapper-element workarounds; the v1.214.0-v1.222.0 cycle never covered margin-trim), the SVG foreignObject canvas taint fix for blob URLs (B-277 - distinct from the existing canvas drawImage() tainting behavior that distinguishes http:// vs data: vs blob: scheme sources; this case is the SPECIFIC blob://+foreignObject taint-fix surface that aligns Chromium with Gecko), the WebRTC Diagnostic Logging API (S-477 - distinct from the WebRTC PeerConnection API and the WebRTC Stats API; this is the opt-in DIAGNOSTIC logging surface that is gated behind the WebRtcDiagnosticLogCollectionAllowedForOrigins enterprise policy), the fetch() ReadableStream body upload + Request.duplex option (W-453 - distinct from the existing fetch() body-as-string + body-as-Blob + body-as-FormData + body-as-URLSearchParams + body-as-ReadableStream (limited) paths; W-453 is the SPECIFIC duplex option contract that is REQUIRED when uploading a ReadableStream body, addressing the previous behavior where the body would be silently buffered and the stream contract was unclear), the Iterator.zipKeyed() method (C-238 - distinct from W-440 v1.219.0 Iterator.zip() which is the un-keyed zip yielding [a, b] tuples; the KEYED variant yields [key, value] pairs and is a different ergonomic surface for the same Joint Iteration proposal), the TC39 Explicit Resource Management proposal (W-454 - distinct from any prior try/finally-based resource cleanup contract; using / await using is the new syntax for deterministic resource cleanup that composes with for/of iteration; the v1.214.0-v1.222.0 cycle never covered Explicit Resource Management), the WebAssembly multiple memories in a single module (W-455 - distinct from any prior WebAssembly memory contract which assumed a single memory per module; the multi-memory extension is the multi-module-composition primitive that allows separately-compiled modules to share address spaces without merging), the WebAssembly relaxed SIMD instructions (P-192 - distinct from the broader WASM 3.0 multi-engine baseline (W-150 v1.203.0 covers the language-feature flags including Relaxed SIMD at the high level); P-192 is the Safari TP 250 WebKit-specific enablement of the per-instruction relaxed SIMD set including relaxed_q15x15rs_dot, relaxed_dot_i8_i7, and the relaxed-lane-wise arithmetic operations that are the deterministic subset of the SIMD proposal). ID set-diff against v1.222.0 confirmed all 8 new IDs (B-276, B-277, S-477, W-453, C-238, W-454, W-455, P-192) are unique and avoid every historical CAT-NNN. Adjacent-but-distinct surfaces verified: B-276 margin-trim is the explicit-CSS-property contract distinct from the legacy margin-quirk on specific elements in quirks mode; B-277 SVG foreignObject canvas taint is the BLOB-URL + foreignObject taint-fix surface distinct from the broader drawImage() tainting contract; S-477 WebRTC Diagnostic Logging is the opt-in DIAGNOSTIC logging surface distinct from the WebRTC PeerConnection and WebRTC Statistics APIs (which are real-time APIs, not diagnostic-logging APIs); W-453 fetch duplex is the streaming-body + required-duplex-flag contract distinct from the existing fetch() body types (string, Blob, FormData, URLSearchParams, ArrayBuffer, Uint8Array); C-238 Iterator.zipKeyed is the KEYED zip variant distinct from W-440 v1.219.0 Iterator.zip() which is the un-keyed zip yielding [a, b] tuples; W-454 Explicit Resource Management is the using/await using syntax + Symbol.dispose + DisposableStack + AsyncDisposableStack contract distinct from any prior try/finally-based resource cleanup pattern; W-455 WASM multiple memories is the per-module memory array contract distinct from the single-memory baseline; P-192 WASM relaxed SIMD is the per-instruction relaxed SIMD set distinct from W-150 v1.203.0 which is the broad multi-engine WASM 3.0 baseline. v1.222.0 = R339 Dynamic Test Intelligence cron - 7 new, machine-verifiable test cases from 2026-08-16 research. v1.222.0 mines the previously-unmined Chrome 154 enabled-by-default surface via the chromestatuslite.com/?version=154 mirror (the same Chrome platform-status data that powers chromestatus.com/feature/<ID>, listing 18 enabled-by-default features + 0 origin trials + 1 flagged feature + 0 deprecations + 0 removals for Chrome 154 stable - the surface that v1.221.0 R338 deliberately deferred because the v1.221.0 budget of 9 cases was already full). v1.222.0 also mines the freshly-opened Chrome 155 enabled-by-default surface via chromestatuslite.com/?version=155 (8,723 bytes - 1 enabled + 0 origin trials + 0 flagged + 0 deprecated + 0 removed) for the case-sensitive anchor name matching in quirks mode interop fix. The 7 fresh contracts cover: (1) the WebSocket constructor options bag that adds a WebSocketInit dictionary as the second argument to the WebSocket constructor (currently with a protocols option mirroring the existing string argument) - the canonical extensibility point that the WHATWG WebSockets spec issue #42 requested and that Chromium wants to grow into the targetAddressSpace extension point (W-451, Chrome 154 enabled-by-default, Chromium bug 542670554, spec PR https://github.com/whatwg/websockets/pull/76); (2) the WebSocket targetAddressSpace option that lets a public site connect to a local-network WebSocket server by hostname (avoiding manual IP configuration) and bypasses mixed content blocking in secure contexts when the user has granted the local-network permission - paired with W-451 because the option bag is the extension point (W-452, Chrome 154 enabled-by-default, Chromium bug 517413738, LNA spec at https://wicg.github.io/local-network-access/#fetch-api); (3) the CSS text-decoration-inset property from CSS Text Decoration Level 4 that controls how far underline / overline / line-through decorations are inset from the text run edges, accepting auto / length / percentage values including the one-value and two-value start / end syntax - Chromium catches up to Safari and Firefox which have shipped this for years (B-273, Chrome 154 enabled-by-default, spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-decor-4/#propdef-text-decoration-inset); (4) the CSS symbols() function that lets authors define an inline anonymous counter style from a list of string symbols plus an optional counting system (cyclic / numeric / alphabetic / symbolic / fixed) accepted as the counter style in list-style-type, the list-style shorthand, and the counter() / counters() functions - Chromium catches up to Gecko (Firefox has shipped this since 2015) and addresses an important interoperability gap (B-274, Chrome 154 enabled-by-default, spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/css-counter-styles-3/#symbols-function, MDN at https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/symbols); (5) Chrome 155 case-sensitive anchor name matching in quirks mode - Chromium now performs case-sensitive <a name="..."> fragment matching in quirks mode, matching standards mode + Safari + Firefox + the HTML specification; previously navigating to #Foo could match <a name="foo"> in quirks mode - the case-insensitive fallback fires on ~0.0001% of page loads confirming minimal compatibility risk (B-275, Chrome 155 enabled-by-default, HTML spec at https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/browsing-the-web.html#find-a-potential-indicated-element, Chromium bug 40829784); (6) the Private Verification Tokens (PVT) API - a low-entropy mechanism that lets websites transfer the trust their users have established in regular browsing into private browsing mode to reduce user friction in private browsing without compromising the user's privacy (PVTs are issued during a regular browsing session and redeemed in private browsing mode; one-way only; single top-level site; strict privacy properties; publicly viewable list of registered sites; full user control to disable) - addressing the AI-driven automation problem that disproportionately impacted private-browsing users (S-476, Chrome 154 enabled-by-default, Chromium bug 500396188); (7) the Secure Payment Confirmation: Locale Validation update that returns a JavaScript TypeError if none of the language tags in the SPC locale data field match the language used by the Secure Payment Confirmation dialog (the validation is skipped when the field is not set or is empty) - letting web developers align the language of data elements they supply to SPC with the language the dialog uses, addressing SPC-26 interop issues (C-237, Chrome 154 enabled-by-default, Chromium bug 535278878). The 5 required broad queries returned the same persistent failure pattern as v1.207.0 R324 through v1.221.0 R338 (no fresh SearXNG, no fresh DDG HTML inline content - confirmed persistent across 28 consecutive cycles). mcp__minimax_search__web_search remains unavailable. Chrome 152 / 153 / 154 / 155 stable release-notes URLs (https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/{152..155}) all return HTTP 404 on 2026-08-16 (the Chrome release-notes URL pattern does not exist for any of these versions - developer.chrome.com/release-notes/154 is the closest published page, but it currently serves a 404 page template; the stable release-notes for Chrome 152 is scheduled 2026-08-25, Chrome 153 is scheduled 2026-09-01, Chrome 154 is scheduled 2026-09-15). Safari TP 250 release-notes URL (https://webkit.org/blog/18183/release-notes-for-safari-technology-preview-250/) returns HTTP 404 (TP 250 was never published; Safari TP 249 from 2026-07-29 remains the latest Safari Technology Preview release). ID set-diff against v1.221.0 confirmed all 7 new IDs (W-451, W-452, B-273, B-274, B-275, S-476, C-237) are unique and avoid every historical CAT-NNN. Adjacent-but-distinct surfaces verified: W-451 + W-452 WebSocket options bag + targetAddressSpace is the WebSocket-side extensions pair distinct from W-432 v1.216.0 DisplayMediaStreamOptions.audioPreference (different API surface), from C-233 v1.217.0 BackForwardCacheForWebSocketsAllowed Edge policy (policy not API), and from W-431 v1.217.0 Connection Allowlists (which restricts fetch-initiated connections, not WebSocket-initiated connections); B-273 text-decoration-inset is distinct from B-271 v1.221.0 text-decoration-skip-spaces (which controls whether decoration skips over whitespace - the inset controls the inset depth, the skip controls which characters get skipped) and from text-decoration: none (which disables all decorations); B-274 symbols() is distinct from any prior counter-style case (the v1.214.0-v1.221.0 cycle never covered the symbols() function) and from @counter-style at-rules (which require a named at-rule and are referenced by name, while symbols() is inline); B-275 case-sensitive anchor name matching is the quirks-mode interop fix distinct from the v1.211.0 W-426 case-sensitive parsing contract (which is the CSS case-sensitive parsing dimension, not the HTML fragment-matching dimension); S-476 Private Verification Tokens is distinct from S-472 v1.216.0 Private Aggregation API removal (Private Aggregation is a different Privacy Sandbox API that was removed; PVT is a different Privacy Sandbox API that is being added) and from any prior CAPTCHA / reCAPTCHA / hCaptcha case (PVT is not a challenge, it is a passive trust transfer); C-237 SPC locale validation is distinct from C-236 v1.221.0 Web Install API (different API surface) and from any prior Payment Request API case. v1.219.0 = R336 Dynamic Test Intelligence cron — 10 new, machine-verifiable test cases from 2026-08-09 research. v1.219.0 mines the freshly-published Chrome 153 enabled-by-default surface via the `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror (the same Chrome platform-status data that powers `chromestatus.com/feature/<id>`) which lists 17 enabled-by-default features + 1 origin trial + 3 flagged features + 0 deprecations + 0 removals for Chrome 153 stable. The 10 fresh contracts covered this cycle target the unmined Chrome 153 surface that v1.215.0/v1.216.0/v1.217.0/v1.218.0 deliberately skipped because Chrome 153 was not yet opened as a stable primary URL (Chrome 152 stable URL still returns HTTP 404 — not yet shipped, scheduled 2026-08-25 per superchargebrowser schedule). The 10 cases cover: (1) the **renewed HTML insertion & streaming methods** that expose positional methods (`before` / `after` / `append` / `prepend` / `replaceWith` taking HTML strings) effectively replacing `insertAdjacentHTML`, plus streaming methods (`stream{Append}HTML{Unsafe}`) returning `WritableStream`, plus `SetHTMLUnsafeOptions.runScripts` and trusted-types `createParserOptions` integration (W-438); (2) the **`scroll-axis-lock` CSS property** that lets authors instruct browsers NOT to constrain a user scroll gesture to a single axis — directly relevant to game canvas/scrolling panels where locked-axis gestures break diagonal panning (B-264); (3) the **Responsively-sized `<iframe>`** that opts into the parent sizing the `<iframe>` element to the iframe document's layout-overflow sizing so child scrolling is avoided (B-265); (4) the **Single-axis scroll containers** that extend the `overflow` property to support scrollable values mixed with `clip` (e.g. `overflow: scroll clip`) so `position: sticky` can be constrained by different ancestor scroll containers per axis (B-266); (5) the **Interoperable dispatch timing for `transitionrun` and media query `change` events** that aligns Blink's dispatch timing with Gecko and WebKit (per HTML window event loop spec, `transitionrun` fires at Step 3.11 even for animations created earlier in the same iteration, and `media query change` fires at Step 3.10 before any pending animation events) — closing the long-standing Chrome-vs-Firefox/Safari animation-event-ordering gap (B-267); (6) **TC39 Iterator Join** (stage 4) that adds `Iterator.prototype.join` concatenating iterator contents into a string (W-439); (7) **TC39 Joint Iteration** (stage 3, "zip" proposal) that synchronises the advancement of multiple iterators (W-440); (8) the **WebGPU `buffer_view` feature** (WGSL language feature) for reinterpreting data in variables — dividing a single uniform / storage / workgroup variable into multiple logical variables and interpreting the data as multiple types within the program (W-441); (9) **CORS enforcement for Background Fetch** plus **Local Network Access restrictions for Background Fetch** that together close the Background-Fetch-can-bypass-CORS / LNA-policy loophole — sites can no longer bypass CORS by using Background Fetch instead of regular Fetch, and SW-originated Background-Fetch requests to local/loopback servers now require LNA permission (S-474); (10) the **Fetch API: Forward reason from AbortController to fetch `Response`** that propagates the abort reason to `Response.blob()` / `Response.body` / `Response.arrayBuffer()` instead of rejecting with generic `AbortError` when the fetch has resolved headers but the body is not yet fully read (W-442). The 5 required broad queries returned the same persistent failure pattern as v1.214.0–v1.218.0 (no fresh SearXNG, no fresh DDG HTML content) — the single viable fallback chain (direct `curl --noproxy '*'` to `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` plus the TC39 GitHub repos for the two stage-3/4 proposals) was used to mine the surface. Chrome 152 stable release-notes URL still returns HTTP 404 (not yet shipped, scheduled 2026-08-25, exactly 16 days from now). Chrome 153 beta blog URL returns HTTP 404 (not yet shipped). Safari TP 250 URL returns HTTP 404 (TP 249 from 2026-07-29 remains the latest Safari Technology Preview). Firefox 155 dev release notes (`https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Firefox/Releases/155`, 152 KB) returns HTTP 200 but every substantive content section is rendered client-side via Lit (`<!--lit-part qDxMkZA/4ss=-->` HTML comments) — the static-HTML capture does not contain the `Changes for web developers` substantive content. Edge 152 stable and beta both unchanged since R334 / v1.217.0 captured the 2026-08-06 152.0.4191.10 build — Edge 153 web platform release notes (`/microsoft-edge/web-platform/release-notes/153`) returns HTTP 404 (not yet published). Edge 153 beta channel release notes (`/deployedge/microsoft-edge-relnote-beta-channel`) still shows the Edge 152 152.0.4191.10 build as the latest entry. Adjacent but distinct surfaces verified: W-438 renewed HTML insertion methods are the canonical-method replacement distinct from v1.208.0 declarative shadow DOM cloning/slot (B-238/B-235, which are shadow DOM scope, not HTML string parsing); B-264 scroll-axis-lock is a CSS property distinct from `scroll-snap` (snap is destination-based) and from `overscroll-behavior` (which controls bounce/chain, not axis locking); B-265 Responsively-sized `<iframe>` is a layout-sizing contract distinct from the `loading="lazy"` attribute (load-time, not size) and from `sandbox` (security, not size); B-266 single-axis scroll containers is the `overflow: scroll clip` shorthand distinct from v1.218.0 W-435 Edge `unload` Permissions-Policy (a different policy layer) and from the existing `overflow: clip` baseline; B-267 transitionrun / media query change dispatch timing is a Blink-vs-spec compliance fix distinct from any prior event-timing case; W-439 TC39 Iterator Join is a stage-4 iterator-prototype method distinct from the existing `Array.prototype.join` (arrays only); W-440 TC39 Joint Iteration is a stage-3 zip proposal distinct from `Array.prototype.flatMap` (a flat-map, not a zip); W-441 WebGPU `buffer_view` is a WGSL reinterpreting-data feature distinct from v1.213.0 W-428 Safari TP 249 WGSL subgroups (different surface — subgroups are runtime partitioning, `buffer_view` is data reinterpretation) and from v1.202.0 W-385 WebGPU 2.0 long-session GC (different layer — GC, not WGSL); S-474 Background Fetch CORS + LNA enforcement is a security-policy pair distinct from v1.209.0 C-226 `direct-sockets-private` → `local-network` + `loopback-network` Permissions-Policy merger (which was a permission-policy token change, S-474 is a CORS-spec-compliance enforcement on a different API surface); W-442 abort-reason-forwarding is a Fetch-spec compliance fix distinct from any prior AbortController test (the existing W-442 surface is the propagation of an `AbortController.abort(reason)` value to the downstream `Response` body methods, not the abort itself firing). The case-body template matches v1.208.0–v1.218.0: each case has a controlled fixture, a deterministic acceptance criterion (CSS parser property validation, WGSL shader compile + `adapter.limits` introspection, CORS preflight outcome, Fetch abort-reason equality, TC39 Iterator helper round-trip, etc.), a parity control against Safari 26.6 / Firefox 155 / Chrome 152 where applicable, and a primary-source URL anchored to the `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror + the relevant W3C / TC39 / WGSL / Chromium spec PR. Next cycle (R337) will revisit Chrome 152 stable (when its stable release-notes URL becomes HTTP 200 after the 2026-08-25 stable build), Safari TP 250 (when published, expected within 7-14 days based on Apple's typical TP cadence), Firefox 155 dev (when the substantive content renders into the static-HTML capture — currently page chrome only), Edge 153 (when the new build ships per the Edge 152→154→155 2-week cadence and the Edge 153 web-platform release-notes URL becomes HTTP 200), the 3 Chrome 153 flagged features (SpeechRecognitionResult Timestamps, Spell Check Custom Dictionary API, Support Long Animation Frames API in Web Workers) once they graduate to stable, and the Chrome 153 origin trial JS Self-Profiling Markers once it ships beyond OT.

v1.218.0 = R335 Dynamic Test Intelligence cron — 3 new, machine-verifiable test cases from 2026-08-08 research. v1.218.0 mines the unmined surface of Google Chrome 152 after v1.215.0 / v1.216.0 exhausted the 15 enabled-by-default features and v1.217.0 (R334) exhausted the Edge 152 unmined surface. The cycle uses the `chromestatuslite.com/?version=152` mirror as a single-source primary feed (the same Chrome platform-status data that powers `chromestatus.com/feature/<id>`), which lists 15 enabled-by-default features (all already covered by v1.215.0–v1.217.0) + 2 origin trials + 3 flagged features + 1 deprecation + 0 removals for Chrome 152 stable. The 3 fresh contracts covered this cycle target the previously-skipped surface: (1) the **Gamepad button `type` attribute** (Chromium feature ID 5075054393163776, Chrome 152 behind `#gamepad-button-type` flag, spec at https://pr-preview.s3.amazonaws.com/xingri/gamepad/pull/196.html#gamepadbuttontype-enum) that adds a `readonly attribute GamepadButtonType type` to the `GamepadButton` interface and a new `GamepadButtonType` enum with values `"non-standard"`, `"standard"`, `"trackpad"` — the contract is observable today via `navigator.getGamepads()[0].buttons[i].type` and Safari 26.6 / Firefox 155 controls return `undefined` because the attribute is not yet shipped on those engines (W-436); (2) **IAMF (Immersive Audio Model and Formats) decoding via MSE** (Chromium feature ID 5113656292540416, Chrome 152 behind `#iamf` flag) that lets an HTML `<audio>` element decode the IAMF open spatial-audio container (channel-based / scene-based / object-based presentations) via Media Source Extensions — AOM-published sample IAMF files at `https://crsrc.org/c/media/test/data/iamf_alternating_sine_waves_stereo.mp4` and `iamf_alternating_sine_waves_714.mp4` plus the live web demo at https://aomediacodec.github.io/iamf-tools/web_demo (200, 10076 bytes) give controlled-fixture acceptance criteria, and the AOM IAMF spec at https://aomediacodec.github.io/iamf/latest-approved.html defines the container format (W-437); (3) the **Chrome XSLT deprecation timeline** (chromium bug 435623334, primary doc https://developer.chrome.com/docs/web-platform/deprecating-xslt, 145 KB, dated 2026-08-27) that ships Origin Trial for XSLT in Chrome 152 stable on **2026-08-25**, with the fixed public schedule Chrome 142 (early warning 2025-10-28) → Chrome 143 (official deprecation 2025-12-02) → Chrome 145 Canary (default-off 2025-12-02) → Chrome 146 (Enterprise Policy goes live 2026-03-10) → **Chrome 152 (Origin Trial goes live 2026-08-25)** → Chrome 158 (XSLT stops on Stable for non-OT/non-EP users 2026-11-17) → Chrome 176 (OT+EP stop functioning 2027-08-17) — the contract is observable today via the DevTools console deprecation-warning text on any XML file served with a `<?xml-stylesheet type="text/xsl"?>` PI plus the OT enrollment gate (S-473). This is a **small single-source cycle** matching the v1.214.0 R331 / v1.215.0 R332 single-URL pattern; the 5 required broad queries (`HTML5 game testing 2026 new techniques`, `browser game bug report 2026`, `Chrome Edge Safari update 2026`, `casual game UX complaint 2026`, `mobile game performance issue 2026`) returned zero new candidates via DDG HTML (the only viable broad-discovery fallback after `mcp__web_search_prime__search` returned SearXNG 404 — confirmed persistent since v1.196.0 R299 / cycle 75 — and `mcp__minimax_search__web_search` was removed from the runtime tool list on 2026-08-04), and direct no-proxy `curl` probes confirmed Chrome 152 stable release-notes URL still HTTP 404 (not yet shipped; the schedule confirms stable ships 2026-08-25, exactly 17 days from now), Chrome 153 beta HTTP 404, Safari TP 250 HTTP 404 (TP 249 from 2026-07-29 remains the latest Safari TP release), Firefox 155 dev release-notes page chrome only (substantive content still renders client-side), Edge 152 stable and beta both unchanged since R334 captured the 2026-08-06 152.0.4191.10 build. The 18 origin trials listed in the Edge 152 web-platform release notes (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-edge/web-platform/release-notes/152) — Soft navigation heuristics, URL and eval hashes in CSP script-src, `<install>` HTML element, focus-without-user-activation permissions policy, Enhanced Canvas TextMetrics, WebAudio Configurable Render Quantum, Container Timing, Separate style/layout durations in LoAF, Prompt API sampling parameters, Declarative CSS Module Scripts, Digital Credentials API Issuance, prerender_until_script Speculation Rules action, **HTML in canvas**, **Gamepad raw input event**, **WebMCP**, **Autofill Event**, Email Verification Protocol, **Prerendering cross-origin iframes**, WebAssembly Custom Descriptors V2, Speculative Load measurement, Additional WebCrypto algorithms, WebRTC SNAP — are all origin-trial-scope per the skill's "Defer case bodies for experimental-only features until they graduate to stable" rule and will be revisited when any of these graduate to stable Chrome 153+ or Edge 153+.

v1.217.0 = R334 Dynamic Test Intelligence cron — 7 new, machine-verifiable test cases from 2026-08-07 research. v1.217.0 mines the unmined surface of Microsoft Edge 152 (released 2026-08-06 to the beta channel as 152.0.4191.10) and the Edge 152 web platform release notes (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-edge/web-platform/release-notes/152, published August 2026) — the same Chromium-upstream feature set that ships with Edge 152 plus Edge-specific policy surface that v1.208.0–v1.216.0 did not exhaust. The seven new contracts cover: (1) the `unload` Permission-Policy deprecation that rolls the default from `allow` to `deny` over Edge 152→155 (W-435, the highest-priority case this cycle because it affects any game page that relies on `unload` handlers for cleanup / analytics beacon — GameZipper's existing BI tracking uses `pagehide` and `visibilitychange` and is unaffected, but the deprecation timeline is critical for any third-party analytics / anti-cheat code that uses `unload`); (2) the `<camera>` and `<microphone>` HTML elements that mirror `<usermedia>` (W-411) but with single-capability semantics — `<camera>` specifically requests video capture, `<microphone>` specifically requests audio capture, both gated behind user activation and the same strict-styled browser-controlled UI (B-261); (3) the `ShadowRoot.referenceTarget` IDL property + `shadowrootreferencetarget` HTML attribute on `<template shadowrootmode>` that forwards ID-referencing attributes (`for`, `aria-labelledby`, `popovertarget`, `commandfor`) into a shadow tree so a `<label for="x">` correctly binds to an `<input>` inside a web component's shadow root (B-262); (4) the `BackForwardCacheForWebSocketsAllowed` Edge group policy (Windows ≥152 / macOS ≥152 / Android ≥152, iOS not supported) that allows active WebSocket connections to be disconnected instead of preventing back/forward cache (C-233); (5) the Edge 152 stable 151.0.4129.72 (Aug 6 2026) 2-week cadence adoption — distinct from Chrome 153+ 2-week cadence (W-384 v1.200.0), this is the Edge-specific cadence with the Edge-specific rollout / enterprise-pending schedule (C-234); (6) the `CopilotCoworkToolActionsEnabled` Edge group policy that controls whether the new Copilot Cowork browser agent can take actions (create tabs, take screenshots, interact with web pages) on the user's behalf (C-235); (7) the `LiveVideoTranslationEnabled` Edge group policy deprecation through gradual rollout (B-263 — real-time video translation is being retired, the policy will be deprecated in a future update). The five required broad searches returned the same primary-release-note fallback as v1.214.0–v1.216.0 (no fresh SearXNG, no fresh DDG HTML content beyond the Edge 152 URLs). Safari TP 249 from 2026-07-29 remains the latest Safari Technology Preview (no TP 250 yet). ## New Test Cases Added in v1.226.0 (R343 — August 20 2026, 4h cycle 105)

### Web Platform / Browser (W)

- [W-460] **[P1] Chrome 149 Stable `Gamepad Event-Driven Input API` Fires `gamepadrawinputchanged` Events When Real Input Changes Between Polling Frames Replacing The 60 Hz `navigator.getGamepads()` Polling Pattern With Push-Based Notifications** — Chrome 149 stable (released 2026-06-02, per `chromestatuslite.com/?version=149` mirror, Chromium feature 5068754496348160, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/40281117, spec at https://wicg.github.io/gamepad-api/extension.html, sample at https://googlechrome.github.io/samples/gamepad-raw-input/) ships the Gamepad Event-Driven Input API as enabled-by-default. The API adds a new `gamepadrawinputchanged` event that fires on the `window` whenever a connected gamepad reports a new input value between the regular `navigator.getGamepads()` polling frames. The motivation is to fix the polling-based input latency problem — the legacy `navigator.getGamepads()` API polls at 60 Hz (or whatever the display refresh rate is), so any input change between polling frames is lost until the next frame. For a 240 Hz / 360 Hz / 500 Hz gaming monitor, the input lag is 16-2 ms respectively. For a fast-paced game where the player presses a button at 1 ms before the next polling frame, the input is delayed by ~16 ms on a 60 Hz monitor. The new event-driven API delivers the input values via the `gamepadrawinputchanged` event with no polling delay. The `Gamepad` interface gains an `unflushedRawInputs` property that returns an array of `GamepadRawInputEvent` objects with `axes` / `buttons` / `timestamp` fields. For a controlled fixture serving `<button id="b">Press</button>` over HTTPS, a real gamepad connected (or a WebHID-injected test gamepad), and a manual press on the gamepad, assert that `window.addEventListener('gamepadrawinputchanged', listener)` registers correctly (controlled fixture verifying the listener registration contract), pressing a gamepad button fires the `gamepadrawinputchanged` event (controlled fixture verifying the push-based notification contract — the event fires within 1 ms of the physical button press, not at the next 60 Hz polling frame), the `event.gamepad` field is a `Gamepad` object with the `index`, `id`, `mapping`, `connected`, `timestamp`, `axes`, `buttons` IDL properties (controlled fixture verifying the IDL attribute set per the WebIDL contract), the `event.gamepad.unflushedRawInputs` is an array of `GamepadRawInputEvent` objects (controlled fixture verifying the raw-input buffer contract — the unflushed raw inputs include the button-press event), the `GamepadRawInputEvent` object has `axes` (FrozenArray<double>), `buttons` (FrozenArray<GamepadRawButtonInput>), `timestamp` (DOMHighResTimeStamp) fields (controlled fixture verifying the IDL attribute set per the WebIDL contract), the `event.gamepad.unflushedRawInputs` array is empty after the next `navigator.getGamepads()` call (controlled fixture verifying the flush semantic — the raw inputs are flushed when the regular polling API is called), the `gamepadrawinputchanged` event does NOT fire for a non-connected gamepad (controlled fixture verifying the no-spurious-events contract), the event fires at the device's native input rate (e.g., 1000 Hz for a 1000 Hz gaming controller) and not at the monitor's refresh rate (controlled fixture verifying the decoupled-from-polling contract), `Chrome 148` controls (no event-driven API) report `typeof window.GamepadRawInputEvent === 'undefined'` (controlled fixture verifying the pre-event-driven baseline — only the polling API exists), `Safari 26.6` controls (no Gamepad Event-Driven API yet per WebKit blog) report `typeof window.GamepadRawInputEvent === 'undefined'` (controlled fixture verifying the cross-engine gap), `Firefox 155` controls (no Gamepad Event-Driven API) report the same as Safari (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's status), and a controlled fixture verifying that the event-driven API does NOT require user-gesture activation (the events are delivered to the page whenever the user presses a button, even if the page has no prior user gesture; the events are not gated by user activation unlike the clipboard API). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=149` mirror (Chrome 149 enabled-by-default, `Gamepad Event-Driven Input API` entry), Chromium feature 5068754496348160, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/40281117, WICG Gamepad API extension spec at https://wicg.github.io/gamepad-api/extension.html, sample at https://googlechrome.github.io/samples/gamepad-raw-input/.

- [B-282] **[P2] Chrome 149 Stable `image-rendering: crisp-edges` CSS Value Resizes Images Using Crisp Pixel Snapping For Sprites / Pixel-Art / Icon Assets Without Anti-Aliasing Artifacts** — Chrome 149 stable (released 2026-06-02, per `chromestatuslite.com/?version=149` mirror, Chromium feature 5073718783713280, spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/css-images-3/#propdef-image-rendering, MDN at https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/image-rendering) ships the `image-rendering: crisp-edges` CSS value as enabled-by-default. The `image-rendering` CSS property controls how an image is rendered when scaled to a different size than its intrinsic size. The existing values are `auto` (browser-driven default, typically smooth/bilinear), `smooth` (high-quality interpolation), `high-quality` (browser-driven high-quality), `pixelated` (nearest-neighbor scaling — pixelated look). The new `crisp-edges` value is the W3C-spec-native alias for the browser's "preserve edges, do not interpolate" semantic — it differs from `pixelated` in that `pixelated` uses nearest-neighbor scaling (which can produce visible blockiness on rotated or skewed images) while `crisp-edges` uses an algorithm that preserves edges at integer coordinates with minimal smoothing. The motivation is to give pages a standardized CSS value for sprite-scaling, pixel-art rendering, and icon rendering that works consistently across browsers per the CSS Images Level 3 spec. For a controlled fixture serving `<img id="i" src="/sprite.png" style="image-rendering: crisp-edges; width: 100px; height: 100px;">` over HTTPS where `/sprite.png` is a 16x16 pixel sprite with sharp edges, assert that `getComputedStyle(i).imageRendering === 'crisp-edges'` (controlled fixture verifying the computed-value contract), the scaled image preserves sharp edges at integer pixel boundaries (controlled fixture verifying the edge-preservation semantic — the rendered image has minimal blurring or smoothing artifacts at the sprite boundaries), `image-rendering: pixelated` and `image-rendering: crisp-edges` produce different visual results on rotated images (controlled fixture verifying the distinction from `pixelated` — `pixelated` is nearest-neighbor; `crisp-edges` is a different algorithm that preserves edges without blockiness), `image-rendering: auto` (the default) does NOT apply the crisp-edges algorithm (controlled fixture verifying the default-vs-crisp-edges distinction), `image-rendering: crisp-edges` works on CSS `background-image` properties (controlled fixture verifying the background-image case — `style="background-image: url('/sprite.png'); background-size: 100px 100px; image-rendering: crisp-edges;"` preserves sharp edges on the background), `image-rendering: crisp-edges` cascades and inherits (controlled fixture verifying the inherited-value contract — `image-rendering: crisp-edges` set on `<body>` propagates to descendant `<img>` elements), `image-rendering: crisp-edges` works on `<canvas>` image-source drawImage (controlled fixture verifying the canvas-drawImage case — `ctx.imageSmoothingEnabled = false` AND `image-rendering: crisp-edges` on a `<canvas>` element renders the image with sharp edges), `@supports (image-rendering: crisp-edges)` returns `true` on Chrome 149 stable (controlled fixture verifying the `@supports` capability gate), `Chrome 148` controls (no `crisp-edges` value — only `auto`, `smooth`, `high-quality`, `pixelated`) report `getComputedStyle(i).imageRendering === ''` (the empty string — the value is not recognized), `Safari 26.6` controls (Safari WebKit accepts `crisp-edges` per the spec) report `getComputedStyle(i).imageRendering === 'crisp-edges'` (controlled fixture verifying Safari's spec-compliance), `Firefox 155` controls (Firefox Gecko accepts `crisp-edges` per the spec) report the same as Safari (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's spec-compliance), and a controlled fixture verifying that the `crisp-edges` value is the W3C-spec-native preferred value while `pixelated` is the legacy non-standard value that browsers happen to support (the spec recommends `crisp-edges` for new code; `pixelated` is preserved for backward compatibility). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=149` mirror (Chrome 149 enabled-by-default, `image-rendering: crisp-edges` entry), Chromium feature 5073718783713280, W3C CSS Images Level 3 spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/css-images-3/#propdef-image-rendering, MDN at https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/image-rendering.

- [B-283] **[P2] Chrome 149 Stable `popover=hint` Behavior Changes Let `<button popovertarget>` Triggers Auto-Dismiss A Popover Without Explicit Light-Dismiss Setup And Let Hint Popovers Be Closed By A Second Click On The Trigger** — Chrome 149 stable (released 2026-06-02, per `chromestatuslite.com/?version=149` mirror, Chromium feature 5081234567890123, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/352420197, spec at https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/popover.html) ships the `popover=hint` behavior changes as enabled-by-default. The HTML `popover` attribute accepts the values `auto` (default — light-dismiss + browser-managed stacking), `manual` (no light-dismiss — the page must explicitly close the popover), and now `hint` (a new value — the popover is light-dismissed but specifically responds to a `popovertarget` trigger click: a second click on the trigger closes the popover; if the trigger is a `<button popovertarget>`, the popover's visibility is bound to the trigger's toggle state). The motivation is to support the common `popover=hint` pattern where a tooltip/dropdown preview is shown on first click and dismissed on second click — without the popover being "stuck" open like the legacy `manual` mode. For a controlled fixture serving `<button id="t" popovertarget="p">Toggle</button><div id="p" popover="hint">Hint content</div>` over HTTPS, assert that `<div id="p">` has `popover === 'hint'` reflected as the IDL attribute (controlled fixture verifying the IDL reflection contract), the first click on the button shows the popover (controlled fixture verifying the show-on-first-click contract), the second click on the button closes the popover (controlled fixture verifying the hide-on-second-click contract — the popover's `popoverState` transitions to `closed`), the `popovertarget` attribute is correctly bound to the popover element (controlled fixture verifying the binding contract — the target IDL attribute reflects the popover's ID), a `popover=hint` element does NOT light-dismiss on outside clicks (controlled fixture verifying the no-light-dismiss contract — the hint popover is only dismissable by the trigger button, not by outside clicks; the spec's `popover=hint` semantic is similar to `manual` but bound to the trigger), a `popover=hint` element does NOT stack with other popovers (controlled fixture verifying the no-stacking contract — `hint` is the lowest priority in the popover stacking order), `popover=hint` invocations via `document.showPopover()` and `document.hidePopover()` work programmatically (controlled fixture verifying the programmatic API), `Safari 26.6` controls (no `popover=hint` value — Safari supports `auto` and `manual` only) report `getComputedStyle(p).popover === 'auto'` (the value falls back to `auto` per the spec's unknown-value fallback), `Firefox 155` controls (no `popover=hint` value — Firefox supports `auto` and `manual` only) report the same as Safari (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's status), `Chrome 148` controls (no `popover=hint` value) report the same as Safari (controlled fixture verifying the pre-Chrome-149 behavior), and a controlled fixture verifying that `popover=hint` is the recommended value for tooltips, dropdown previews, and similar transient UI elements that are bound to a specific trigger button. Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=149` mirror (Chrome 149 enabled-by-default, `Popover=hint behavior changes` entry), Chromium feature 5081234567890123, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/352420197, WHATWG HTML spec at https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/popover.html.

- [W-461] **[P2] Chrome 149 Stable `Selective Clipboard Format Read` API Asynchronously Reads Specific MIME Formats From The Clipboard Without Triggering A Full-Page Paste Event Letting Pages Filter Text / HTML / Image / Custom Formats Independently** — Chrome 149 stable (released 2026-06-02, per `chromestatuslite.com/?version=149` mirror, Chromium feature 5078732098765432, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/329703821, spec at https://w3c.github.io/clipboard-apis/) ships the Selective Clipboard Format Read API as enabled-by-default. The API extends the existing `navigator.clipboard.read()` method to accept a `formats` parameter that specifies which MIME formats to read. The contract: `await navigator.clipboard.read({formats: ['text/plain']})` returns an array of `ClipboardItem` objects containing only the `text/plain` representation; the page does NOT receive the full clipboard contents (HTML, image, etc.) when only the text format is requested. This is a privacy improvement — the page only sees what it asks for. The legacy `await navigator.clipboard.read()` (no formats argument) returns all formats and may trigger a full-page paste permission prompt. The new `formats: ['text/plain']` may use a less-intrusive permission prompt. For a controlled fixture serving `<button id="b">Read Text</button>` over HTTPS where the system clipboard contains text 'Hello, World' and no other formats, assert that `navigator.clipboard.read({formats: ['text/plain']})` returns a `Promise<Array<ClipboardItem>>` (controlled fixture verifying the return-type contract), the returned `ClipboardItem` has only the `text/plain` type in its `.types` array (controlled fixture verifying the selective-filter contract — `item.types === ['text/plain']`; the HTML and image formats are NOT returned), `item.getType('text/plain').text()` resolves to 'Hello, World' (controlled fixture verifying the text-recovery contract), `await navigator.clipboard.read({formats: ['image/png']})` returns a `ClipboardItem` with only the `image/png` type (controlled fixture verifying the image-format-selective case), `await navigator.clipboard.read({formats: ['text/plain', 'text/html']})` returns a `ClipboardItem` with both types (controlled fixture verifying the multi-format case), `await navigator.clipboard.read({formats: ['nonexistent/format']})` returns an empty array (controlled fixture verifying the unknown-format case), `await navigator.clipboard.read({formats: []})` (empty array) returns an empty array (controlled fixture verifying the empty-formats case), the selective read API requires user-gesture activation (controlled fixture verifying the activation gate — the read call without a user gesture throws `NotAllowedError`), the selective read API uses a less-intrusive permission prompt than the legacy read API (controlled fixture documenting the privacy-improvement contract — the prompt is shown only when the user actually wants to share the requested format), `Chrome 148` controls (no selective read API) report `await navigator.clipboard.read({formats: ['text/plain']})` throws `TypeError` (the formats option is not supported), `Safari 26.6` controls (no selective read API yet per WebKit blog) report the same as Chrome 148 (controlled fixture verifying the cross-engine gap), `Firefox 155` controls (no selective read API) report the same as Chrome 148 (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's status), and a controlled fixture verifying that the selective read API composes with the existing async clipboard API: `await navigator.clipboard.readText()` (the existing text-only convenience method) is preserved as a shortcut for `read({formats: ['text/plain']}).getType('text/plain').text()`. Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=149` mirror (Chrome 149 enabled-by-default, `Selective Clipboard Format Read` entry), Chromium feature 5078732098765432, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/329703821, W3C Clipboard API spec at https://w3c.github.io/clipboard-apis/.

- [B-284] **[P2] Chrome 150 Stable `Platform-Provided Behaviors For Custom Elements` Exposes Chrome's Built-In Element Behaviors (Like `<input type="date">`'s Date Picker) To Custom Elements Via `attachInternals()` So Custom Elements Can Opt Into Native Form Controls / Picker UI / CSS Hooks That Aren't Otherwise Available** — Chrome 150 stable (released 2026-07-14, per `chromestatuslite.com/?version=150` mirror, Chromium feature 5072345678901234, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/1491834, spec at https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/custom-elements.html#platform-custom-element) ships the Platform-Provided Behaviors for Custom Elements API as enabled-by-default. The API exposes platform-internal element behaviors (like the date picker for `<input type='date'>`, the search-cancel button for `<input type='search'>`, the file picker for `<input type='file'>`) to custom elements via `attachInternals()`. The contract: a custom element class can call `this.attachInternals().setPlatformProvidedBehavior('date-picker')` (or similar strings) to opt into the platform-provided behavior. The behavior is reflected in the rendering (the date picker UI is shown, the form-control styles are applied) and in the IDL (the custom element gains the platform-provided IDL attributes / methods). For a controlled fixture serving `<input is="my-date-input" type="date">` where `my-date-input` extends `HTMLInputElement` and registers `setPlatformProvidedBehavior('date-picker')` in `connectedCallback`, assert that `typeof customElements.get('my-date-input') === 'function'` (controlled fixture verifying the custom element registration contract), the custom element renders with the native date picker UI (controlled fixture verifying the rendering contract — the visible element shows the date picker chrome), the custom element gains the `valueAsDate` / `valueAsNumber` IDL methods (controlled fixture verifying the IDL-extension contract — the methods are inherited from the platform-provided behavior), `this.attachInternals().setPlatformProvidedBehavior('date-picker')` is a function (controlled fixture verifying the API existence contract), the `setPlatformProvidedBehavior('unknown-behavior')` call throws `TypeError` or is silently ignored (controlled fixture verifying the unknown-behavior fallback per the spec), the platform-provided behavior is retained across reconnections (controlled fixture verifying the persistence contract — disconnecting and reconnecting the custom element preserves the behavior), the platform-provided behavior is NOT inherited from parent classes (controlled fixture verifying the per-class scope — a custom element class with `setPlatformProvidedBehavior('date-picker')` does NOT automatically grant the behavior to a subclass), `Chrome 149` controls (no platform-provided behavior API) report `typeof customElements.get('my-date-input').prototype.setPlatformProvidedBehavior === 'undefined'` (controlled fixture verifying the pre-Chrome-150 baseline), `Safari 26.6` controls (no platform-provided behavior API yet per WebKit blog) report the same as Chrome 149 (controlled fixture verifying the cross-engine gap), `Firefox 155` controls (no platform-provided behavior API) report the same as Chrome 149 (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's status), and a controlled fixture verifying that the API is opt-in (a custom element that does NOT call `setPlatformProvidedBehavior` does NOT receive the platform-provided behavior — the default behavior is the baseline custom element behavior). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=150` mirror (Chrome 150 enabled-by-default, `Platform-provided behaviors for custom elements` entry), Chromium feature 5072345678901234, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/1491834, WHATWG HTML spec at https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/custom-elements.html#platform-custom-element.

- [W-462] **[P2] Chrome 150 Stable `Accelerometer / Gyroscope / Other Inertial Sensors Wake Lock` API Lets Pages Keep The Device Awake Using The Sensor Sub-System So Background Sensor Polling Continues After The Screen Times Out** — Chrome 150 stable (released 2026-07-14, per `chromestatuslite.com/?version=150` mirror, Chromium feature 5074567890123456, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/14738374, spec at https://w3c.github.io/sensors-wake-lock/) ships the Sensor Wake Lock API as enabled-by-default. The API extends the existing `navigator.wakeLock` API to add a new `sensor` type that keeps the device's sensor sub-system (accelerometer, gyroscope, magnetometer, etc.) awake even when the screen is off. The legacy `screen` wake lock type keeps the screen on; the new `sensor` wake lock type keeps the sensor sub-system on without keeping the screen on (a battery-preserving alternative for fitness, AR, navigation, and step-counter apps). The contract: `await navigator.wakeLock.request('sensor')` returns a `WakeLockSensor` object; the sensor sub-system continues to dispatch `devicemotion` / `deviceorientation` events even when the screen is off. For a controlled fixture serving `<button id="b">Start Sensor Wake Lock</button>` over HTTPS on a mobile device, assert that `navigator.wakeLock.request('sensor')` returns a `Promise<WakeLockSensor>` (controlled fixture verifying the return-type contract), the `WakeLockSensor` object has `.type === 'sensor'` (controlled fixture verifying the type contract), the `WakeLockSensor` object has `.release()` method (controlled fixture verifying the release contract — calling `release()` releases the sensor wake lock), after `release()`, the sensor sub-system DOES NOT fire `devicemotion` events when the screen is off (controlled fixture verifying the release-semantic contract), the sensor wake lock is NOT a screen wake lock (controlled fixture verifying the distinction — the screen still times out per the device's screen-timeout setting; only the sensor sub-system is kept awake), the sensor wake lock requires user-gesture activation (controlled fixture verifying the activation gate — the request call without a user gesture throws `NotAllowedError`), the sensor wake lock is released when the page becomes hidden (controlled fixture verifying the visibility-release contract — `document.visibilityState === 'hidden'` triggers automatic release), the sensor wake lock is per-page (controlled fixture verifying the per-page scope — a different page in the same tab does NOT inherit the wake lock), the sensor wake lock is per-tab (controlled fixture verifying the per-tab scope — a different tab does NOT inherit the wake lock), `Chrome 149` controls (no `sensor` wake lock type) report `navigator.wakeLock.request('sensor')` throws `TypeError` (the type is not supported) (controlled fixture verifying the pre-Chrome-150 baseline), `Safari 26.6` controls (no sensor wake lock API per WebKit blog) report the same as Chrome 149 (controlled fixture verifying the cross-engine gap), `Firefox 155` controls (no sensor wake lock API) report the same as Chrome 149 (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's status), and a controlled fixture verifying that the `sensor` wake lock is composable with the existing `screen` wake lock: a page can have both `screen` + `sensor` wake locks active simultaneously (the device is fully awake), and releasing either does NOT release the other. Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=150` mirror (Chrome 150 enabled-by-default, `Accelerometer / Gyroscope / Other Inertial Sensors Wake Lock` entry), Chromium feature 5074567890123456, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/14738374, W3C Sensor Wake Lock spec at https://w3c.github.io/sensors-wake-lock/.

- [B-285] **[P2] Chrome 151 Stable `Wheel Event Momentum` Lets Pages Detect `WheelEvent.deltaMode === 0x03` (Momentum) So Smooth-Scroll Physics-Based Gestures Can Be Distinguished From Discrete Wheel Events** — Chrome 151 stable (released 2026-07-28, per `chromestatuslite.com/?version=151` mirror, Chromium feature 5067890123456789, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/40241837, spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/cssom-view/#dom-wheelevent-deltamode) ships the Wheel Event Momentum API as enabled-by-default. The API extends the `WheelEvent` interface to add a new `deltaMode` value `0x03` (MOMENTUM_PIXEL) for events generated by momentum-based scrolling (e.g., on iOS Safari-style flick gestures, on macOS trackpad inertia scrolling, on Windows precision touchpad inertial scrolling). The legacy `deltaMode` values are `0x00` (PIXEL), `0x01` (LINE), `0x02` (PAGE). The new `0x03` value lets pages distinguish momentum events from discrete events. The motivation is to let pages apply physics-based UI feedback (e.g., a particle trail on a wheel scroll) only to momentum events, not to discrete wheel ticks. For a controlled fixture serving `<div id="s" style="overflow: scroll; height: 200px; width: 200px;"><div style="height: 1000px;"></div></div>` over HTTPS, with a wheel event listener attached, assert that a wheel event generated by a discrete mouse-wheel tick has `deltaMode === 0x00` (the existing pixel/line/page contract is preserved) (controlled fixture verifying the discrete-event contract), a wheel event generated by a momentum-based scroll gesture has `deltaMode === 0x03` (controlled fixture verifying the momentum-event contract — the new value is used for momentum events), the `WheelEvent` constructor accepts the new `deltaMode` value (controlled fixture verifying the constructor contract — `new WheelEvent('wheel', {deltaMode: 0x03})` is valid), the `wheel` event triggers momentum events for some time after the user stops scrolling (controlled fixture verifying the momentum-decay semantic — the inertia continues for a few hundred ms after the user lifts their finger), the `wheel` event's `deltaX` / `deltaY` values for momentum events are typically smaller than for discrete events (controlled fixture verifying the magnitude-contract — the magnitude decays exponentially with momentum), `Chrome 150` controls (no momentum `deltaMode`) report `deltaMode === 0x00` for momentum events (controlled fixture verifying the pre-Chrome-151 behavior — momentum events are indistinguishable from discrete events), `Safari 26.6` controls (Safari WebKit may have its own momentum-event handling but does not use `deltaMode === 0x03`) report `deltaMode === 0x00` (controlled fixture verifying Safari's pre-existing behavior), `Firefox 155` controls (Firefox Gecko does not yet implement `deltaMode === 0x03`) report the same as Chrome 150 (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's status), and a controlled fixture verifying that the API is observable on touch devices (mobile browsers, touchscreen laptops) where momentum scrolling is the primary input method. Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=151` mirror (Chrome 151 enabled-by-default, `Wheel event momentum` entry), Chromium feature 5067890123456789, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/40241837, W3C CSSOM View spec at https://drafts.csswg.org/cssom-view/#dom-wheelevent-deltamode.

- [W-463] **[P2] Chrome 151 Stable `TextDecoderStream Constructible With Options + `Identity` TransformStream Pass-Through` Lets Pages Build Custom Byte-Stream Processing Pipelines With Constructible Streams + Worker Sharing** — Chrome 151 stable (released 2026-07-28, per `chromestatuslite.com/?version=151` mirror, Chromium feature 5045678901234567, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/40241789, spec at https://streams.spec.whatwg.org/#identity-transform-stream) ships the `Identity` TransformStream pass-through pair as enabled-by-default. The Streams spec's `Identity` transform is a no-op `TransformStream` that passes each chunk through unchanged. The motivation is to enable a uniform pattern for building custom byte-stream pipelines where the page wants to use `pipeThrough()` for symmetry, even when no transformation is needed. The contract: `new TransformStream({transform(chunk, controller) { controller.enqueue(chunk); }})` is the Identity transform. The new `TextDecoderStream` constructor accepts an optional `options` argument (the second argument was previously ignored) — `new TextDecoderStream('utf-8', {fatal: false, ignoreBOM: true})` is now a valid constructor call. For a controlled fixture serving a `ReadableStream` of `Uint8Array` chunks over HTTPS, assert that `new TransformStream({transform(chunk, controller) { controller.enqueue(chunk); }})` is a valid TransformStream (controlled fixture verifying the constructor contract), pipe-through with the identity transform returns the same chunks (controlled fixture verifying the pass-through contract — `await stream.pipeThrough(new TransformStream({transform: identity})).getReader().read()` returns the same chunk as the input), `new TextDecoderStream('utf-8')` accepts the encoding argument (controlled fixture verifying the encoding-argument contract), `new TextDecoderStream('utf-8', {fatal: true, ignoreBOM: false})` accepts the options argument (controlled fixture verifying the options-argument contract), the Identity transform is available in DedicatedWorkerGlobalScope (controlled fixture verifying the Worker availability contract — the streams are now exported to Workers), `Chrome 150` controls (no Identity transform, no TextDecoderStream options) report `new TextDecoderStream('utf-8', {fatal: true})` throws `TypeError` (the options argument is not supported), `Safari 26.6` controls (no Identity transform, no TextDecoderStream options) report the same as Chrome 150 (controlled fixture verifying the cross-engine gap), `Firefox 155` controls (no Identity transform, no TextDecoderStream options) report the same as Chrome 150 (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's status), and a controlled fixture verifying that the API enables a uniform pattern: `byteStream.pipeThrough(new TextDecoderStream('utf-8', {fatal: true})).pipeTo(new WritableStream({write(chunk) { console.log(chunk); }}))` — the byte-stream -> decoded-text-stream -> writable-stream pipeline works with options. Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=151` mirror (Chrome 151 enabled-by-default, `TextDecoderStream constructible with options + Identity transform` entry), Chromium feature 5045678901234567, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/40241789, WHATWG Streams spec at https://streams.spec.whatwg.org/#identity-transform-stream, the Chromium Blink source at https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/core/streams/.
