## New Test Cases Added in v1.212.0 (R329 — August 2 2026, 4h cycle 91)

### Browser Compatibility / CSS (B)

- [B-240] **[P1] Safari 27 Beta Customizable `<select>` `appearance: base-select` Plus `::picker-icon`, `::checkmark`, and `<selectedcontent>` Lets `<select>` Be Restyled Without Losing Native Form Semantics** — Safari 27 beta (WWDC26, released 2026-06-08, WebKit changes between 316531@main…317820@main plus TP 249 fixes) adds the customizable `<select>` surface: `appearance: base-select` clears the native styling, new pseudo-elements `::picker-icon` and `::checkmark` target the disclosure indicator and the per-option checkmark, and the new `<selectedcontent>` element inside the button displays the currently selected option's content. For a controlled `<select>` with `<option value="a">Alpha</option><option value="b">Bravo</option>` rendered with `appearance: base-select; ::picker-icon { content: "▾"; color: red; } ::checkmark { content: "✓"; } ::picker(select)::part(option) { background: yellow; }`, assert `getComputedStyle(select).appearance === "base-select"`, `select.querySelector("selectedcontent").textContent === "Alpha"` after programmatically setting `select.value = "a"`, the `::picker-icon` pseudo-element can be matched via `select.matches("::picker-icon")` and a controlled `getComputedStyle(select, "::picker-icon").content` reads the custom string, the `::checkmark` pseudo-element on the open picker matches the selected option and `getComputedStyle(option, "::checkmark").content === "✓"`, the same controlled `<select>` still submits `value="a"` to a `<form>` containing it, keyboard navigation (ArrowDown / Enter) still cycles through options and emits a `change` event, a CSS-disabled control (`select.disabled = true`) keeps `appearance: base-select` but prevents the picker from opening, Firefox 153 control (no customizable-select styling yet, parser-only per v1.202.0 W-386) renders the native picker and ignores `appearance: base-select`, and Chrome 150 / Safari 26.6 controls (no `base-select`) keep the platform-native rendering. Source: WebKit News from WWDC26 — WebKit in Safari 27 beta, Jun 8 2026, https://webkit.org/blog/17967/news-from-wwdc26-webkit-in-safari-27-beta/ and Safari Technology Preview 249, https://webkit.org/blog/18182/release-notes-for-safari-technology-preview-249/.

- [B-241] **[P1] Safari 27 Beta `:heading` Pseudo-Class Matches `<h1>` Through `<h6>` as a Single Selector and Combinable With Functional Selectors** — Safari 27 beta adds the `:heading` pseudo-class (CSS Selectors Level 5) that matches any heading element `<h1>` through `<h6>`. For a controlled document containing `<h1>A</h1><h2>B</h2><h3>C</h3><h4>D</h4><h5>E</h5><h6>F</h6><p>G</p><div>H</div>`, assert `document.querySelectorAll(":heading").length === 6` and the matched set equals the six heading elements in tree order, `document.querySelectorAll("h1, h2, h3, h4, h5, h6").length === 6` returns the same set under the equivalent old-style selector, `:heading:not(h3)` matches exactly 5 elements (all except the `<h3>`), `:is(:heading) p` returns no descendants (headings don't nest), the `CSS.supports("selector(:heading)")` returns `true` in Safari 27 beta and `false` in Safari 26.6 / Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 controls, a controlled `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector(":heading")).display` returns `"block"` (the user-agent style) for each matched heading, the `:heading` selector does not match `<hgroup>` wrappers, `<header>` elements, or `<div role="heading">` (different mechanism), and Safari TP 246 control (no `:heading`) returns `document.querySelectorAll(":heading").length === 0`. Source: WebKit News from WWDC26 — WebKit in Safari 27 beta, https://webkit.org/blog/17967/news-from-wwdc26-webkit-in-safari-27-beta/.

- [B-242] **[P2] Safari 27 Beta `width: stretch` / `height: stretch` / `min-width: stretch` / `min-height: stretch` Replaces `100%` and `-webkit-fill-available` With Margin-Aware Fill-Available Sizing** — Safari 27 beta adds the `stretch` keyword for box-sizing properties: `width: stretch`, `height: stretch`, `min-width: stretch`, `min-height: stretch`, etc. The keyword makes the element fill the available space in the relevant axis while accounting for margins, replacing both `width: 100%` (which ignores margins and overflows when margins are applied) and the long-standing `-webkit-fill-available` workaround. For a controlled `<div class="card">` inside a `<section>` of width 600 px with `section { padding: 0; } .card { width: stretch; margin-inline: 20px; }`, assert `getComputedStyle(card).width === "560px"` (600 − 20 − 20, margin-aware), `getComputedStyle(card).getPropertyValue("width") === "560px"`, the same `.card { width: 100%; margin-inline: 20px; }` control overflows by 40 px (computed `width: 600px`, box left-edge at -20 px), the same `.card { width: -webkit-fill-available; margin-inline: 20px; }` control matches `stretch` behavior (560 px), `min-width: stretch` on a child of a flex column does not collapse the child's box to zero (the fix is margin-aware in both axes), `CSS.supports("width: stretch")` returns `true` in Safari 27 beta and `false` in Safari 26.6 / Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 controls, and the `stretch` keyword is rejected on `max-width` (still `none` / `<length>` / `<percentage>` / `min-content` / `max-content` / `fit-content`). Source: WebKit News from WWDC26 — WebKit in Safari 27 beta, https://webkit.org/blog/17967/news-from-wwdc26-webkit-in-safari-27-beta/.

- [B-243] **[P2] Safari 27 Beta `contain: style` Applies to CSS Quotes and Scopes Quote Effects to a Subtree** — Safari 27 beta extends `contain: style` so it now applies to CSS `content` quote levels (the `open-quote` / `close-quote` / `no-open-quote` / `no-close-quote` keywords that consume nesting levels via `quotes`). For a controlled `<div class="scope">` with `div.scope { contain: style; quotes: "«" "»" "‹" "›"; }` and `<div.scope><p>before<q>inside<q>deep</q></q>after</p></div>`, assert the outer `<q>` inside `.scope` renders as «inside…», the inner `<q>` renders as ‹deep› (both levels consumed), the same fixture without `contain: style` consumes both quote levels globally (the inner `‹` / `›` would still appear correctly but the outer scope's quoting cannot be re-entered on demand), a controlled sibling `.scope` element after the first one starts fresh at the outer `«` / `»` level (no global quote-level leakage), `CSS.supports("(contain: style)")` returns `true` (Safari 27 beta also stabilizes `contain: style` for general use), `contain: style` does not affect computed font-size / margin / padding (no `contain: layout` side effect), and Safari 26.6 / Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 controls (no quote-level containment) leak the inner level into any subsequent `<q>` outside the scope. Source: WebKit News from WWDC26 — WebKit in Safari 27 beta, https://webkit.org/blog/17967/news-from-wwdc26-webkit-in-safari-27-beta/.

### Web Platform / Browser (W)

- [W-423] **[P1] Safari 27 Beta `Temporal.PlainDate` / `Temporal.ZonedDateTime` / `Temporal.Now` Provide Calendar-Accurate Date Arithmetic Distinct From Timezone-Naive `Date`** — Safari 27 beta (TC39 Temporal proposal, Stage 3, ES2025 candidate) adds the global `Temporal` object with `Temporal.PlainDate`, `Temporal.PlainTime`, `Temporal.PlainDateTime`, `Temporal.ZonedDateTime`, `Temporal.Duration`, and `Temporal.Now`. For a controlled fixture asserting the calendar-accurate arithmetic, construct `const d1 = Temporal.PlainDate.from("2026-01-31")`, `const d2 = d1.add({months: 1})` (returns `2026-02-28`, not `2026-03-03` which is what `Date` arithmetic produces), `const d3 = Temporal.PlainDate.from("2024-02-29").add({years: 1})` (returns `2025-02-28`, leap-year aware), `Temporal.PlainDate.from("2026-02-29")` rejects `RangeError` (no Feb 29 in 2026), `Temporal.Now.zonedDateTimeISO("America/Los_Angeles")` returns a `Temporal.ZonedDateTime` whose `hour` matches the local hour in that zone (not UTC) for a fixed fixture time, `Temporal.Duration.from("P1Y2M3DT4H5M6S")` round-trips through `toString()` byte-equal to the input, `Temporal.PlainDate.compare(d1, d2) === -1` for d1 < d2, and `Date.now()` (legacy) returns the same epoch ms as `Temporal.Now.instant().epochMilliseconds` (within ±1 ms). The same arithmetic with `Date` controls returns `2026-03-03` (the overflow bug) for the `+1 month` operation and `2025-03-01` for the leap-year case, proving Temporal fixes real bugs. Safari 26.6 / Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 controls return `Temporal === undefined` and throw `ReferenceError` on construction, falling back to a `Date`-based path without calendar correction. Source: WebKit News from WWDC26 — WebKit in Safari 27 beta, https://webkit.org/blog/17967/news-from-wwdc26-webkit-in-safari-27-beta/.

- [W-424] **[P2] Safari 27 Beta `popover="hint"` Auto-Dismisses on Outside Click and Stays Separate From Auto-Popover Hint Toggle** — Safari 27 beta adds the `popover="hint"` value (HTML bug 129495028, announced in Safari TP 249 release notes) for a hint-style popover that auto-dismisses on an outside click and stays independent from auto/manual popovers (only one hint can be open at a time, but a hint and an auto can coexist). For a controlled `<button popovertarget="hint">Show hint</button><div id="hint" popover="hint">Hint text</div>` fixture, assert `document.querySelector("[popover=hint]").popover === "hint"` and `el.matches(":popover-open")` toggles correctly when the button is activated, clicking outside the hint (any element not inside the hint) closes it within 50 ms and emits a `toggle` event with `oldState: "open"`, opening a second `popover="hint"` while the first is open auto-closes the first (single-hint invariant), a coexisting `<div popover="auto">Auto</div>` does not auto-close the hint and vice versa, `el.showPopover()` / `el.hidePopover()` methods work on hint popovers (the popover API is shared), the hint is positioned via CSS anchor positioning when an `anchor` attribute is set, and Safari 26.6 / Chrome 150 controls (no `popover="hint"`) treat the attribute as invalid and fall back to `popover="auto"` (the legacy default). Firefox 153 control renders the hint per its own hint-popover contract (v1.202.0 W-392, single-open + focus restore) which is distinct from Safari 27 beta's auto-dismiss-on-outside-click behavior. Source: Safari Technology Preview 249 release notes, https://webkit.org/blog/18182/release-notes-for-safari-technology-preview-249/ (HTML bug 129495028).

- [W-425] **[P2] Safari 27 Beta WGSL `clip_distances` Built-In Lets Vertex Shaders Define Custom Clipping Planes for Game Rendering** — Safari 27 beta adds the WebGPU `clip_distances` built-in value to WGSL shaders, allowing vertex shaders to define custom clipping planes that discard geometry on one side of an arbitrary plane before rasterization. For a controlled WebGPU pipeline fixture that creates a shader module with `@vertex fn vs(@builtin(vertex_index) idx: u32) -> @builtin(position) vec4f { var out: vec4f; out = in_positions[idx]; out.z = ... ; if (out.z > clip_distance_plane) { out.clip_distances[0] = -1.0; } return out; }` (using the new `clip_distances` array built-in), assert the WGSL parser accepts `clip_distances[0]` as a built-in attribute without a parser error (control WGSL without `clip_distances` parses cleanly but discards the assignment as a no-op), the rendered pipeline produces a framebuffer where pixels behind the clip plane are absent (assert `gpuCanvasContext.getCurrentTexture().createView()` then read back a uniform-color region outside the clip-plane viewport), `WebGPU.clip_distances` is a recognized WGSL built-in in Safari 27 beta (`WGSL.validate("clip_distances")` returns no error), the GPUDevice queryable limits include the relevant `clipDistances` constant (`adapter.limits`), Safari 26.6 / Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 controls return a WGSL parser error on `clip_distances` (built-in not registered), and the deprecated `clip_position` built-in (legacy) still works in the same pipeline as a regression check. Source: WebKit News from WWDC26 — WebKit in Safari 27 beta, https://webkit.org/blog/17967/news-from-wwdc26-webkit-in-safari-27-beta/ (WebGPU section, `clip_distances` built-in).

The unmined Safari 27 beta surfaces used this cycle were: customizable `<select>` `appearance: base-select` + `::picker-icon` + `::checkmark` + `<selectedcontent>` (B-240); the `:heading` pseudo-class (B-241); `width: stretch` / `height: stretch` keyword for box-sizing (B-242); `contain: style` extending to CSS quotes (B-243); the `Temporal` object with `Temporal.PlainDate` / `Temporal.ZonedDateTime` / `Temporal.Now` (W-423); and WGSL `clip_distances` built-in for WebGPU (W-425). ## New Test Cases Added in v1.211.0 (R328 — August 1 2026, 4h cycle 90)

### Browser Compatibility / CSS (B)

- [B-219] **[P2] Chrome 151 `ruby-overhang` `auto` / `spaces` / `none` Aliased `spaces` Lets Ruby Annotations Overhang Only Whitespace and CJK Punctuation** — Chrome 151 (bug 366873207) adds the `ruby-overhang` CSS property with three keywords: `auto`, `spaces`, and `none`. Per the CSS Working Group specification, `none` is aliased to `spaces`, so both `none` and `spaces` permit ruby annotation text to overhang only whitespace and CJK punctuation, never a CJK base character. `auto` allows the user-agent's default (no overhang over any base character). For a controlled East-Asian fixture using `<ruby>漢<rt>かん</rt>字<rt>じ</rt></ruby>` paired with two non-CJK punctuation-adjacent spans, assert `getComputedStyle(rt).rubyOverhang === "spaces"` when the property is `none` (the document default), `getComputedStyle(rt).rubyOverhang === "spaces"` when the property is explicitly `spaces`, `getComputedStyle(rt).rubyOverhang === "auto"` when the property is `auto`, the spanned whitespace element's `getBoundingClientRect().right` is preserved (no glyph overstrike) under `spaces` and `none`, the same fixture under `auto` keeps the whitespace span's measured width within ± 0.5 px of the property-default baseline, and Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls return `rubyOverhang === "auto"` regardless of the property value (these browsers do not implement `ruby-overhang`), and a `chrome://flags/#enable-experimental-web-platform-features` disabled control still returns `auto`. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [P-184] **[P2] Chrome 151 Cross-Origin Redirect Timing Opt-In Server Directive Reports Cross-Origin Redirect Latency to the Destination Origin** — Chrome 151 (bug 521861828) enables servers to opt in to having their cross-origin redirects measured by the destination origin of a navigation. A redirect target that includes the `Supports-Loading-Mode: fenced-frame` and `Timing-Allow-Origin: <destination>` headers (or the equivalent `redirect-timing-opt-in` response directive) returns timing data accessible to the destination origin. For a controlled HTTPS fixture where `/redirect-source` returns HTTP 302 with `Location: /destination` plus the directive, and `/destination` returns a page that immediately reads `performance.getEntriesByType("navigation")`, assert the resulting `PerformanceNavigationTiming` entry has `redirectStart > 0`, `redirectEnd > redirectStart`, `redirectCount === 1`, and the entry's `serverTiming` array contains the opt-in directive; the same fixture without the directive returns `redirectStart === 0` or `redirectEnd === 0` (timing suppressed), a same-origin redirect returns full timing regardless of the directive, and Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls either ignore the directive and return full timing (Firefox 153 partial support) or omit the directive entirely (Safari 26.6). Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [P-185] **[P2] Chrome 151 Navigation: Ignore Duplicate Navigations Prevent An Identical New Navigation From Cancelling the In-Flight One** — Chrome 151 (bug 366060351) prevents an in-flight navigation from being cancelled by an identical new navigation that is initiated in rapid succession. For a controlled HTTPS fixture that returns a 1.5 MB body with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` and a 200 ms inter-chunk delay, assert that firing `location.href = "/destination"` twice within 50 ms produces exactly one `navigation` PerformanceEntry (not two), the single entry's `requestStart` is the timestamp of the **first** click (the second click is suppressed), the `pageshow` event fires once with `event.persisted === false`, a controlled anchor-click that lands on a different URL (e.g. `/destination?v=2`) immediately cancels the first navigation and produces a new entry (the dedup is exact-URL only), and Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls produce two `navigation` entries under the same 50 ms double-click (the duplicate is not yet deduped). The bug is observable on slow networks where double-click on a search button wastes one full navigation request; under the new behavior only the first request reaches the server. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

### Compatibility / Embedded Runtime (C)

- [C-227] **[P1] Chrome 151 Declarative Performance Observer HTTP Response Header Ships Browser-Resident Telemetry Across Network Failures and Renderer Crashes** — Chrome 151 (Origin Trial, bug 505208781) introduces a declarative HTTP response header-based Performance Observer that reports telemetry from navigation initiation to page termination, even when the request fails due to network errors or the renderer process is terminated by the OS. For a controlled HTTPS fixture that sets the literal `Performance-Observer: <json-config>` header on a 200 response, with a valid Origin Trial token, navigate and then deliberately kill the renderer via DevTools. Assert a `PerformanceObserver` callback fires for `paint`, `largest-contentful-paint`, `layout-shift`, and `navigation` entry types, the original page's `navigator.sendBeacon()` to the telemetry endpoint records the entries' `startTime` and `entryType` byte-equal to the in-page observer, the header's `endpoint` URL receives a beacon POST whose body contains `entryType:"layout-shift"` and `value:0.1` (≥ 0.1 to pass the POOR threshold), a navigation-time `4xx` response still emits a beacon with `entryType:"navigation"` and `responseStatus:404`, and Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls (no `Performance-Observer` header support) emit zero beacons because the header is treated as a custom response header rather than a directive. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [C-228] **[P1] Chrome 151 Prefetch Activation Beacon `on-prefetch-activation` HTTP Response Header Reports Every Prefetch Navigation Use to a Telemetry Endpoint** — Chrome 151 (Origin Trial, bug 499814382) introduces the `on-prefetch-activation` HTTP response header that lets servers specify a telemetry endpoint that the browser notifies whenever a prefetched resource is used for a navigation. For a controlled HTTPS fixture that sets `Link: </destination>; rel=prefetch; as=document` on the index page, and `/destination` returns a `200 OK` response with the `on-prefetch-activation: https://telemetry.example/beacon` header (and a valid Origin Trial token), assert that the subsequent `location.href = "/destination"` navigation results in a `POST` beacon to `https://telemetry.example/beacon` whose `fetch` body contains `{entryType: "prefetch-activation", navigationUrl: "/destination", prefetchSource: "link-prefetch"}`, the beacon fires once per prefetch use (not twice for a back/forward navigation), a non-prefetched navigation to `/destination` does not produce a beacon, the beacon survives a renderer kill (telemetry payload includes `rendererKilled: true` if the renderer is terminated), and Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls never dispatch the beacon because the header is treated as a custom response header. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [C-229] **[P1] Edge 151 `ProcessIsolationEnabled` / `AddressBarClipboardSuggestEnabled` / `EdgeReadingModeServiceBasedExtractionEnabled` Group Policies Are Recognized on Windows ≥ 151 and Embedded WebView2 Hosts Must Accept Procurement-Delivered Values** — Edge 151 (released 2026-07-31, build 151.0.4129.59) introduces three managed-policy toggles that any embedded WebView2 host or GameZipper-hosted Edge inside an enterprise must accept from procurement. For a controlled fixture running Edge 151 with the policy keys set via the registry or `msedgewebview2.admx`, assert (a) `ProcessIsolationEnabled` (Windows ≥ 151, mandatory, requires browser restart) returns true on `chrome://policy` and the `Browser process` row in `edge://process-internals` shows process isolation entries for each running tab; setting the policy to `0` halts the rollout and resets the row, and Chrome 151 (= Edge 151 = Chromium 151) without the policy set reports the gradual rollout as `ProcessIsolationEnabled: not configured`. Assert (b) `AddressBarClipboardSuggestEnabled` (Windows ≥ 151, macOS ≥ 151, mandatory + recommended, requires browser restart) appears on `edge://policy` and the address bar's suggestion dropdown includes a copy-paste of the clipboard content; disabling the policy removes the entry. Assert (c) `EdgeReadingModeServiceBasedExtractionEnabled` (Windows ≥ 151, macOS ≥ 151, mandatory, dynamic refresh supported) appears on `edge://policy` and Reading Mode sends the page text to the extraction service (asserted via a stub `EdgeReadingMode` service endpoint hit during a controlled reading-mode invocation). Edge 150 and Chrome 150 controls return `policy not recognized` for all three, and Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls (different policy namespaces) ignore the policy keys entirely without emitting a console error. Source: Edge 151 release notes, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-relnote-stable-channel and Edge Policy Documentation, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-policies/.

### Sources and Research Notes

- Required broad queries ran on 2026-08-01: `HTML5 game testing 2026 new techniques`, `browser game bug report 2026`, `Chrome Edge Safari update 2026`, `casual game UX complaint 2026`, and `mobile game performance issue 2026`. Results were noisy, dominated by generic mobile-game pages, stale browser/CVE stories, and unrelated troubleshooting content.
- The primary Chrome 151 release notes supplied the maintained unmined bank for 5 of 6 cases: https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151. Direct no-proxy retrieval was used to verify the release-note contracts (rendered HTML saved to `/tmp/chrome-151.html`, 111 KB, last-updated 2026-07-28 UTC) before drafting. The unmined Chrome 151 surfaces used this cycle were: CSS `ruby-overhang` (bug 366873207), cross-origin redirect timing opt-in (bug 521861828), navigation duplicate-navigation cancellation (bug 366060351), Declarative Performance Observer Origin Trial (bug 505208781), and Prefetch Activation Beacon Origin Trial (bug 499814382). All five were already advertised in v1.208.0 R325 source inventory but received no dedicated test case body in cycles 87–89.
- Edge 151 stable release notes (2026-07-31, build 151.0.4129.59) supplied the 6th case: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-relnote-stable-channel. The three new policies (`ProcessIsolationEnabled`, `AddressBarClipboardSuggestEnabled`, `EdgeReadingModeServiceBasedExtractionEnabled`) are the second half of the Edge 151 policy wave; the first half (sidebar retirement, NonMicrosoftAccountSignInEnabled, Intune MAM, OnBulkDataEntryEnterpriseConnector / OnFileAttachedEnterpriseConnector / OnPrintEnterpriseConnector) was noted in the Edge 151 release notes but most policies are non-blocking for the GameZipper hosted experience and do not require a test case.
- Semantic-dedup note: full pre-insert scans of `CHANGELOG.md` and `/home/junze/.openclaw/workspace/COMPLETED.md` found no prior test-case body for `ruby-overhang` keyword semantics, cross-origin redirect timing opt-in, duplicate-navigation cancellation, Declarative Performance Observer response header, `on-prefetch-activation` response header, or the three Edge 151 policy toggles. The Chrome 151 source inventory in v1.208.0 lists `ruby-overhang` only as a mention; the rest are explicitly new contracts not previously surfaced. `selectedcandidatepairchange` appears in v1.207.0 W-408 and the Firefox 154 beta notes; this cycle does not re-cover it. Adjacent-but-distinct surfaces verified: cross-origin redirect timing opt-in (P-184) is distinct from Resource Timing interim/final header split (v1.198.0 P-174); duplicate-navigation cancellation (P-185) is distinct from Navigation preload script overlap (v1.205.0 W-393); `Performance-Observer` header (C-227) is distinct from `prefetch-activation` header (C-228) and from `textStream()` (v1.209.0 W-416); Edge 151 group policies (C-229) are distinct from `direct-sockets-private` permission policy (v1.209.0 C-226) and from WebView2 `DowngradeVersion` (v1.206.0 C-223); `ruby-overhang` (B-219) is distinct from ruby-overhang mention in v1.208.0 source list (no body case).
- Search-quality note: the five required broad queries were all noisy. The three follow-up queries (`Chrome 151 release notes new features full list web platform July 2026`, `Chrome 151 Speculation rules form_submission prerender CSS ruby-overhang declarative performance observer`, `Edge 151 ProcessIsolationEnabled reading mode service-based extraction clipboard suggest`) returned dry content for the first (real Chrome 151 release-notes URL is the type-supplier) and stale content for the other two. mcp__web_search_prime__extract returned 404 for the dedicated docs sub-pages (`/docs/web-platform/declarative-performance-observer`, `/docs/web-platform/prefetch-activation-beacon`) — those Origin Trial pages are not yet publicly written. ## New Test Cases Added in v1.210.0 (R327 — July 31 2026, 4h cycle 89)

### Web Platform / Browser (W)

- [W-417] **[P1] Chrome 151 Speculation Rules `form_submission` Prerender Activates Only a Matching Real GET Form Navigation** — On a same-origin HTTPS fixture, inject `{"prerender":[{"urls":["/fixtures/search?q=arcade"],"form_submission":true}]}` through a `script[type="speculationrules"]`, serve `/fixtures/search` with a prerender-safe page that records `PerformanceNavigationTiming.activationStart`, and submit `<form method="get" action="/fixtures/search"><input name="q" value="arcade"></form>` through a trusted automation click. Assert the destination URL is exactly `/fixtures/search?q=arcade`, its navigation entry has a non-zero `activationStart`, and its form-visible `q` value is `arcade`; a same fixture with `form_submission:false` reaches the same URL with `activationStart === 0`, a `prefetch` rule carrying `form_submission:true` is rejected through the captured speculation-rules parse error, and Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls perform the ordinary GET navigation with no activation. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [W-418] **[P1] Chrome 151 WebCrypto Origin Trial `CHACHA20-POLY1305` Authenticates Controlled Game-State Bytes and Rejects Invalid AEAD Inputs** — With a valid Chrome 151 Origin Trial token, import a fixed 32-byte raw key as `{name:"CHACHA20-POLY1305"}` with `encrypt` and `decrypt` usages; encrypt a fixed game-state byte fixture using a fixed 12-byte IV and AAD, then decrypt it. Assert the decrypted bytes equal the fixture exactly, changing either one ciphertext byte or the AAD makes `decrypt()` reject, and non-extractability makes `exportKey("raw", key)` reject; 31-byte-key, 11-byte-IV, and `tagLength:96` controls reject rather than encrypting. Chrome 150, Firefox 153, and Safari 26.6 controls reject this Origin-Trial algorithm cleanly instead of silently substituting AES-GCM. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [W-419] **[P1] Chrome 151 WebCrypto Origin Trial X-Wing Hybrid KEM `MLKEM768-X25519` Encapsulates and Decapsulates the Same Secret Without Reusing W-414 ML-KEM Derivation** — With the Origin Trial token present, generate a non-extractable `{name:"MLKEM768-X25519"}` key pair with `encapsulateBits` and `decapsulateBits` usages, call `crypto.subtle.encapsulateBits()` with its public key, and pass the returned ciphertext to `crypto.subtle.decapsulateBits()` with its private key. Assert both returned shared-secret `ArrayBuffer` values are byte-identical, the public key reports `algorithm.name === "MLKEM768-X25519"`, and exporting the non-extractable private key rejects; an `ML-KEM-768` public-key control is rejected by the hybrid encapsulation call, proving this is not W-414's ML-KEM-only `deriveBits` / `deriveKey` surface. Chrome 150, Firefox 153, and Safari 26.6 controls reject the hybrid algorithm rather than producing a fallback secret. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [W-420] **[P1] Chrome 151 WebRTC Data Channel SCTP Negotiation Acceleration Origin Trial Places `a=sctp-init` in Both SDP Legs** — With a valid Origin Trial token on both same-origin peer fixtures, create a data channel before `createOffer()`, exchange the offer and answer between two controlled `RTCPeerConnection` instances, and complete ICE locally. Assert the data-channel media section of both SDP strings contains one base64-valued `a=sctp-init:` line, the channel fires `open`, and a fixed payload is echoed byte-for-byte; replacing the offered attribute value with invalid base64 causes `setRemoteDescription()` to reject. A token-free Chrome 151 fixture and Chrome 150 / Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls still open and echo the ordinary data channel but contain no `a=sctp-init:` line in either SDP, rather than claiming accelerated negotiation. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [W-421] **[P2] Chrome 151 `WheelEvent.momentum` Is a Read-Only Boolean and Lets Input Handlers Separate Inertial Fling Events** — In a controlled scroll-surface fixture, assert `WheelEvent.prototype` has a getter-only `momentum` property, `new WheelEvent("wheel", {deltaY:1}).momentum === false`, and `new WheelEvent("wheel", {deltaY:1, momentum:true}).momentum === true`; dispatch both events through the same game-scroll handler and assert it records the physical-control event but ignores the momentum-marked event without changing the selected item. On the hardware-integrated trackpad/precision-touchpad runner, capture a fling trace and assert events after the physical scroll ends are marked `momentum === true` while pre-release events are false. Chrome 150, Firefox 153, and Safari 26.6 controls feature-detect the absent property and use the no-momentum fallback without reading or assigning an undefined field. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [W-422] **[P2] Chrome 151 `FontFaceSet` Global Exposure After `[LegacyNoInterfaceObject]` Removal Keeps `document.fonts` Usable but Rejects Construction** — On a clean same-origin document, assert `window.FontFaceSet` is a global function, `document.fonts instanceof FontFaceSet`, and its `check()` / `ready` members remain usable on a controlled local `FontFace`; assert `new FontFaceSet()` throws `TypeError` and does not replace or mutate `document.fonts`. Chrome 150 is the negative control: `document.fonts` remains usable but `window.FontFaceSet` is absent; Firefox 153 and Safari 26.6 are cross-engine controls where the global exists and construction likewise throws, rather than exposing a constructible unrelated set. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

## New Test Cases Added in v1.209.0 (R326 — July 30 2026, 4h cycle 88)

### Web Platform / Browser (W)

- [W-414] **[P1] Chrome 151 (Origin Trial) `SubtleCrypto.deriveKey` and `deriveBits` Support ML-KEM-768 / ML-KEM-1024 Post-Quantum Key Encapsulation with Byte-Identical Shared Secret to RFC 9180 HPKE** — Chrome 151 (Origin Trial, July 2026) extends `SubtleCrypto` with `deriveKey` and `deriveBits` for `ML-KEM-768` and `ML-KEM-1024` (formerly Kyber768 / Kyber1024) key-encapsulation mechanisms as required by FIPS 203 / NIST PQC round 3. For a fixture that imports an ML-KEM-768 encapsulation key (a 1184-byte public key), a sender and recipient both running on Chrome 151, and an ML-KEM-1024 control (1568-byte public key), assert `crypto.subtle.deriveBits({name: "ML-KEM-768", publicKey: encapsulationKey}, sharedKeyBuffer, 32)` returns a non-null 32-byte `ArrayBuffer`, the recipient's `decapsulate({name: "ML-KEM-768", privateKey}, ciphertext)` returns a `BufferSource` of the same 32 bytes (byte-identical to the sender's output), `deriveKey` with `name: "AES-GCM", length: 256` produces a non-exportable 256-bit `CryptoKey`, repeated encapsulations against the same public key produce distinct 32-byte shared secrets (KEM randomness), `extractable: false` keys fail `exportKey` with `InvalidAccessError`, a wrong-algorithm negative control (`{name: "ML-DSA-65", publicKey: mlDsaKey}`) throws `OperationError`, and Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls reject the algorithm with `AlgorithmError`. Source: Chrome 151 release notes Origin Trial section, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

- [W-415] **[P1] Chrome 151 `LanguageDetector.detect()` Splits Chinese Into Distinct `zh-Hans` and `zh-Hant` Confidence Outputs** — Chrome 151 splits the `LanguageDetector` API `detect()` output so Simplified Chinese text resolves to `zh-Hans` and Traditional Chinese text resolves to `zh-Hant` rather than both returning a generic `zh` or `zh-CN`. For a fixture that initializes `const detector = await LanguageDetector.create({expectedInputLanguages: ["zh-Hans", "zh-Hant"]})`, assert `await detector.detect("今天天气很好,我吃了一碗面")` returns an array whose first element is `{detectedLanguage: "zh-Hans", confidence: ≥ 0.85}`, the same call on Traditional Chinese input `["今天天氣很好,我吃了一碗麵"]` returns `{detectedLanguage: "zh-Hant", confidence: ≥ 0.85}`, mixed input containing both scripts returns the dominant language (within ± 0.10 confidence of the script's share), a `LanguageDetector` constructed with `expectedInputLanguages: ["zh"]` falls back to the same `zh-Hans` / `zh-Hant` outputs (not the legacy merged bucket), Chrome 150 control returns `zh` / `zh-CN` for both inputs (legacy merged bucket), and Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls (where `LanguageDetector` is absent) throw a clean `ReferenceError` rather than returning a merged bucket. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and confirmation, https://so.html5.qq.com/page/real/search_news?docid=70000021_8566a699b6900652.

- [W-416] **[P2] Chrome 151 `Request` / `Response` / `Blob` `textStream()` Returns a `ReadableStream` of `Uint8Array` Chunks Distinct from `body.getReader()` and `arrayBuffer()`** — Chrome 151 adds `textStream()` (and a parallel `bytesStream()`) on `Request`, `Response`, and `Blob` that returns a `ReadableStream` whose chunks are `Uint8Array` (textStream wraps each chunk into a `string` via `TextDecoder`). For a fixture `fetch('/fixtures/large-text.json')` returning a 1.2 MB JSON body, assert `response.textStream()` returns a `ReadableStream` whose first `reader.read()` resolves to `{value: Uint8Array, done: false}`, the underlying `response.bodyUsed === false` after the textStream reader is created and is `true` after the stream completes, sum of `chunk.byteLength` across all reads equals the original `response.headers.get('content-length')` to the byte, the stream can be cancelled mid-read with `reader.cancel()` and a subsequent `response.text()` rejects with `TypeError("Body already consumed")`, a parallel `response.blob()` taken on the original `Response.clone()` returns byte-identical bytes, and Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls (no `textStream`) return `undefined` and fall back to `response.text()` or `response.body` without throwing. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

### Browser Compatibility / CSS (B)

- [B-237] **[P2] CSS Container Queries with `container-type: size` and `container-name` Reflow Game Panel Sub-Containers Without Re-Laying Out Outer Document** — For a controlled panel inside an in-game inventory grid where the panel is its own `@container` (`container-type: size; container-name: inv`) and contains a `@container (min-width: 320px)` rule that switches a 2-column grid to 4-column, assert the computed `containerType` is `"size"`, the computed `containerName` is `"inv"`, the `@container (min-width: 320px) .card { grid-template-columns: repeat(4, 1fr) }` rule applies when the panel's content-box width reaches 320 px and does not apply below 320 px, sibling panels whose container name does not match `inv` are not affected, the panel's layout change does not trigger a document-level `PerformanceObserver` `layout-shift` entry (sub-container reflow only), `CSS.supports("(container-type: size)")` returns `true` in Chrome 151 / Edge 151 / Safari 26.6 / Firefox 153 and the same `@supports` selector without a value (`@supports (container-type: size)`) matches, and a control element without `container-type: size` set still receives viewport-driven media-query rules. Source: Baseline 2026 Container Queries rollout, https://web.dev/cq-stable and Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

- [B-238] **[P2] Chrome 151 Declarative Shadow DOM `<template shadowrootclonable>` Attribute Clones the Declarative Shadow Root on `<use>` Element Deep-Clone** — Chrome 151 (bug 519573765, related to v1.208.0 `shadowrootslotassignment`) adds a `shadowrootclonable` attribute on declarative-shadow-root `<template>` so the cloned declarative shadow root is re-instantiated on every `<use>` deep-clone rather than collapsed to a single shared `ShadowRoot`. For a controlled `<svg>` with `<defs><template id="tpl" shadowrootmode="open" shadowrootclonable shadowrootslotassignment="manual">…</template></defs>` and a `<use href="#tpl"/>` plus a `<use href="#tpl"/>` repeat, assert `template.getAttribute("shadowrootclonable") === ""`, both `<use>` instances expose a distinct `element.shadowRoot` whose `slotAssignment === "manual"`, the slot content of each clone is independently mutable (`use1.shadowRoot.querySelector(".slot-1").textContent = "alpha"` does not change `use2.shadowRoot.querySelector(".slot-1").textContent`), a control without `shadowrootclonable` (only `shadowrootmode="open"`) shares one `ShadowRoot` between the two `<use>` instances (the pre-151 behavior), and Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls (where the attribute is unknown) keep the shared-`ShadowRoot` behavior. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

- [B-239] **[P2] Chrome 151 `<dialog>` Element `closedby` Attribute Adds `"closerequest"` and `"any"` Modal-Escape Behaviors for In-Game Modals** — Chrome 151 adds a `closedby` attribute to `<dialog>` with values `"none"` (default, platform default close behavior), `"closerequest"` (close only when the user explicitly invokes a close action — Escape key, `dialog.requestClose()`, form submit with `method="dialog"`), and `"any"` (also close on backdrop click / outside pointerdown). For a controlled in-game settings modal fixture, assert `dialog.closedBy === "closerequest"` after `dialog.setAttribute("closedby", "closerequest")`, dispatching `new KeyboardEvent("keydown", {key: "Escape"})` on the dialog closes it (returning `dialog.open === false`), dispatching a `pointerdown` on the document but outside the dialog does **not** close it under `closedby="closerequest"` but does close it under `closedby="any"`, calling `dialog.requestClose("cancel")` fires a `close` event whose `event.reason === "cancel"` (or `"close"` for an OK submit), a `cancel` event is cancellable via `event.preventDefault()` only when `closedby !== "none"`, and a non-Chrome-151 control reads `dialog.closedBy === "none"` regardless of the attribute. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

### Performance (P)

- [P-183] **[P2] Chrome 151 `soft-navigation` + `LongAnimationFrame` (LoAF) Co-Emit on Multi-Frame Interactive SPA Transitions; Frame Stalls Stay Below 50 ms on Warm Cache** — Complement to v1.208.0 P-182 and earlier P-170 (LoAF) / P-176 (WebGPU 2.0 timing): on Chrome 151, a controlled SPA that pushes a new view with `history.pushState` plus a heavy `requestAnimationFrame` chain (60 frames of 12 ms each) plus a sub-task-heavy `MessageChannel.postMessage` should emit one `soft-navigation` PerformanceEntry plus zero `long-animation-frame` entries when the warm cache is used. Assert `performance.getEntriesByType('soft-navigation')` includes the new URL with `startTime ≈ Date.now()` within 50 ms, `performance.getEntriesByType('long-animation-frame').filter(e => e.startTime >= softNav.startTime)` is empty, a cold-cache control (`reload()` after `caches.delete()`) emits exactly one LoAF entry with `duration ≥ 50 ms` and `renderStart - startTime ≤ 16.7 ms`, `interaction-contentful-paint` is reported only on the cold-cache path, and Firefox 153 / Safari 26.6 controls (no `soft-navigation` PerformanceEntry) emit zero of either type but the LoAF entry on cold cache. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151, MDN `Performance_API/Long_animation_frame_timing`, https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Performance_API/Long_animation_frame_timing, and v1.208.0 P-182, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151.

### Security / Worker Delivery (S)

- [S-471] **[P0] CVE-2026-56155 Microsoft AD FS Elevation-of-Privilege Active In-The-Wild Exploitation (CVSS 8.0, Jul 15 2026 Patch Tuesday)** — Microsoft disclosed CVE-2026-56155 on 2026-07-15 Patch Tuesday as an AD FS elevation-of-privilege vulnerability with active in-the-wild exploitation, CVSS ~8.0; it allows a low-privileged AD FS-adjacent user to obtain administrative tokens on the federation service, enabling SSO token forging and downstream identity-impersonation against every relying-party application. GameZipper's enterprise SSO path (any hosted-game session that delegates to AD FS, any signed-in user that lands on a corporate-issued device whose AD FS tenant issues tokens, and any save-state / leaderboard endpoint that accepts AD-FS-issued bearer tokens) inherits the risk until the federated AD FS host is patched. For every federated-SSO entry point in GameZipper's hosted-game surface, assert (1) the local AD FS / Azure-AD-Connect tenant is at the 2026-07-15 Patch Tuesday (or later) security baseline by querying `https://<adfs-host>/adfs/ls/?version=1.0` and parsing the `Server` header for the post-patch build identifier, (2) the federated-SSO endpoint is rejected when the inbound `Authorization: Bearer` JWT carries a pre-patch `iss` claim (the federation trust is rotated to a new signing key), (3) any cached AD-FS-issued token older than 24 h is rejected on next request (`401 Unauthorized` + a `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token"`), (4) GameZipper's session-validation endpoint logs every AD-FS-issued token claim and audits the `wids` / `groups` / `aud` against a short-allowlist of expected game-side roles (no `Domain Admins` / `Enterprise Admins` are honored), and (5) managed-mobile users are prompted to re-authenticate on first post-patch login with a fresh device-posture assertion. Source: Microsoft July 2026 Patch Tuesday disclosure, Jul 15 2026, https://www.joeseanor.com/ (2026-07-15), CVE-2026-56155 advisory, https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2026-56155.

### Compatibility / Embedded Runtime (C)

- [C-226] **[P1] Chrome 151 `direct-sockets-private` Merges Into `local-network` and `loopback-network` Permission Policies — Embedded WebView Hosts Must Audit Any Direct TCP/UDP Socket Path** — Chrome 151 (bug 519573765) merges the prior `direct-sockets-private` permission policy into the broader `local-network` and `loopback-network` policies, removing the dedicated `direct-sockets-private` opt-in. For a controlled WebView2 host that uses `navigator.serviceWorker.controller` and the `DirectSockets` API to open a private TCP connection to `127.0.0.1` plus a public-NAT TCP connection, assert the effective `Permissions-Policy` response header (or `document.featurePolicy`) on Chrome 151 contains `local-network` and `loopback-network` entries but **no** `direct-sockets-private` entry, the same `Permissions-Policy: direct-sockets-private=()` header sent by the host is parsed but ignored (no permission granted, no console warning), a request via `new TCPSocket("127.0.0.1", 8080)` now requires `local-network=(self)` in the policy and a `loopback-network` allow, the same request against a public IP requires only `local-network=(self)`, the host's CSP `connect-src` continues to gate the actual socket open, and Chrome 150 control honors the legacy `direct-sockets-private` opt-in. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

### Web Platform / Browser (W)

- [W-410] **[P1] Chrome 151 `AnimationEvent.animation` and `TransitionEvent.animation` Reflect the Triggering `Animation` Object Read-Only** — Chrome 151 (stable Jul 28 2026) adds a read-only `animation` accessor to both `AnimationEvent` and `TransitionEvent` returning the associated `Animation` object that fired the event (bug 40929813). For a controlled `<div>` with `animation: foo 1s` and a parallel element with `transition: opacity 0.5s`, dispatch synthetic `animationstart`/`animationend`/`transitionend` via `el.dispatchEvent(new AnimationEvent('animationend', {animationName: 'foo'}))` and `new TransitionEvent('transitionend')`. Assert `event.animation` is a valid `Animation` whose `effect.target === el`, `playState === 'finished'` after `animationend`, the same Animation identity round-trips through `getAnimations()`, the transition event's `animation` getter returns a non-null `Animation` with `animationName === event.propertyName`'s transition, and Chromium before 151 / Firefox / WebKit controls (where the property is `undefined`) still tolerate the legacy access pattern. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

- [W-411] **[P1] Chrome 151 `<usermedia>` Declarative Capability Element Gates `getUserMedia` Behind a Click Activation Gesture** — Chrome 151 ships the `<usermedia>` declarative permission capability as the MVP (bug 443013457). Render a fixture page with `<button id="fallback">` plus `<usermedia id="cam" audio mediadevices></usermedia>` whose `srcObject` getter is observable; assert the element renders a browser-controlled control, `img`/`canvas` readback is impossible before any user activation, the element's first user activation fires a controlled permission prompt, the resolved `MediaStream` matches the active constraints, the legacy imperative `navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({audio: true})` path still requires its own permission flow and never silently merges with the declarative element, and Chromium before 151 / Firefox / Safari controls either degrade to `HTMLUnknownElement` or fire a no-op event without granting access. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

- [W-412] **[P1] Chrome 151 `DeviceOrientationEvent.requestPermission()` / `DeviceMotionEvent.requestPermission()` Is a Static Promise-Returning API Distinct from Geolocation** — Chrome 151 (bug 947112) adds `DeviceOrientationEvent.requestPermission()` and `DeviceMotionEvent.requestPermission()` static methods that return a Promise resolving to `"granted"` or `"denied"`. For a controlled mobile-shape fixture, assert both static methods exist as functions on the corresponding constructor, calling them without any user gesture throws a `SecurityError` (permission-policy contract), a single user-gesture call resolves exactly once to a stable string, the granted result enables the `deviceorientation` and `devicemotion` event listeners to fire within 500 ms of the resolved promise, denial leaves both event types silent for the rest of the page lifetime, and Firefox / Safari controls (where this static API is absent) either ignore the call or throw a `TypeError` rather than returning a stale state. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

- [W-413] **[P1] Chrome 151 Web Speech `SpeechRecognition.unspokenPunctuation` Lets the Engine Inject Punctuation from Natural Pauses** — Chrome 151 (bug 514764702) adds a boolean `unspokenPunctuation` attribute to the `SpeechRecognition` interface. For a fixture utterance that contains a natural pause but no spoken punctuation (e.g. "hello world how are you [pause] fine thanks"), construct a `SpeechRecognition`, set `unspokenPunctuation = true`, start the session, await a controlled PCM stream, and assert the resulting `SpeechRecognitionResult` final transcript contains either `?`, `,`, or `.` inserted at the pause boundary matching the engine's prosody heuristic, the same utterance under `unspokenPunctuation = false` returns a transcript without injected punctuation, the audio speech context remains closed (`audioContext.state === "closed"`) after the recognition lifecycle, and Firefox (no Web Speech) plus Safari (off-by-default Web Speech) controls never emit transcripts inside this fixture. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

### Browser Compatibility / CSS (B)

- [B-233] **[P1] Chrome 151 `position-anchor` Initial Value Changes from `none` to `normal`, Aligning with Safari and Firefox** — Chrome 151 (ChromeStatus entry linked, no number) changes the initial value of the `position-anchor` CSS property from `none` to `normal`. The `normal` value is equivalent to `none` when `position-area` is `none`, and equivalent to `auto` otherwise. For a controlled `<div>` with `position: absolute` and `position-area: top` applied but no `position-anchor` declared, assert `getComputedStyle(el).positionAnchor === 'normal'` in Chrome 151, the resolved anchor allows the element to remain visible within its anchored position-area box (no `none`-regression on first paint), explicitly setting `position-anchor: none` still produces the `position-area: none` fallback path, Chrome 150 control returns the legacy `none`, and Safari 26.6 + Firefox 154 controls already report `normal`. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

- [B-234] **[P1] Chrome 151 `AnimationTrigger` `play` / `play-forwards` / `play-backwards` Actions Do Not Auto-Rewind a Completed Animation** — Chrome 151 (bug 519573765) removes the legacy auto-rewind behavior so invoking any `play*` action on an already-finished animation no longer restarts it from time 0. For a controlled `<div>` with `@trigger foo --trigger "click" {timeline: scroll()}` and an `Animation` of 200 ms that has reached `playState === "finished"`, dispatch a synthetic `trigger` activation, then capture the `Animation`'s `currentTime` and the rendered frame's hash, assert it is byte-identical to the post-finished state (no restart, no rewind), a subsequent manual `animation.play()` from page code does still restart normally, the `play-forwards` and `play-backwards` actions keep their directional contract, and Safari / Firefox controls that still rewind on completed animations reproduce the pre-151 bug. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

- [B-235] **[P1] Chrome 151 Declarative Shadow DOM `<template shadowrootslotassignment>` Reflects as `HTMLTemplateElement.shadowRootSlotAssignment`** — Chrome 151 (bug 493315747) adds a `shadowrootslotassignment` attribute on declarative-shadow-root `<template>`. Render `<template shadowrootslotassignment="manual">` inside a Declarative Shadow DOM host fixture, parse it via the declarative shadow DOM pipeline, and assert the resolved declarative shadow root has `slotAssignment === "manual"`, `template.shadowRootSlotAssignment === "manual"`, unslotted nodes are rendered exactly where their slot fallback is, switching the attribute to `"named"` (or removing it) restores the `named` slot assignment, the `attachShadow({slotAssignment: "manual"})` imperative control remains valid, and Safari / Firefox controls (which still treat the unknown attribute as Reflecting-attribute-no-clobber) keep the slots assigned by the legacy `slot` element. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

- [B-236] **[P1] Chrome 151 Memory-Safe Rust XML Parser for `DOMParser`, `XMLHttpRequest.responseXML`, and Standalone / External SVG Without XSLT** — Chrome 151 (bug 466303347) migrates the non-XSLT XML parser to a memory-safe Rust implementation. For a controlled fixture serving an XML string via `DOMParser` and the same string via `XMLHttpRequest.responseXML`, parse `<?xml version="1.0"?><root><child attr="v"/></root>` plus a malicious payload containing 256-deep nested entities, deep attribute bombs, and an external DTD; assert the parsed `Document` has identical `childElementCount`, `nodeName`, `tagName`, and `XMLSerializer().serializeToString()` round-trip bytes compared with the previous libxml parser, an XML document with `<?xml-stylesheet type="text/xsl"?>` is still routed to the legacy XSLT parser (control), a 50 MB standalone SVG image loads with `Image()` and renders to a canvas without unbounded memory growth (heap delta ≤ 8x the parsed-string size), and a same-payload WebKit / Firefox control produces the same parse tree and the same SVG pixel hash so regressions in the Rust migration are detectable. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

### Performance / SPA Tracking (P)

- [P-182] **[P1] Chrome 151 `soft-navigation` and `interaction-contentful-paint` PerformanceEntry Types Capture SPA INP and Same-Document Navigation** — Chrome 151 (bug 1338390) adds `soft-navigation` and `interaction-contentful-paint` PerformanceEntry types to track interaction-driven performance in single-page applications. For a controlled SPA fixture with a `history.pushState` triggered by an interactive button, assert `performance.getEntriesByType('soft-navigation')` contains one entry whose `name` is the post-push URL, `startTime` reflects the interaction timestamp, a subsequent interaction within the new view produces an `interaction-contentful-paint` entry whose `startTime < 250 ms` (Google INP good threshold), a same-document hash change does not generate a new `navigation` entry, the entries survive across `performance.measure()` boundaries, and a non-Chrome-151 control produces zero entries of either type. Source: Chrome 151 release notes, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://chromestatus.com/feature/519573765.

### Compatibility / Embedded Runtime (C)

- [C-225] **[P1] Chrome 151 / Edge 151 macOS 12 (Monterey) Support End Forces `navigator.userAgentData`/UA-String Detection Fallback for Embedded WebView Hosts** — Chrome 151 stable no longer supports macOS 12 (Monterey); Chrome 150 was the last release with support; Edge 150 already sunset the same OS in its policy docs. For a controlled macOS 12 host running a WebView2 or embedded chrome fixture, assert the `navigator.userAgentData.brands` array still contains Chromium-style entries but the host runs without security updates (browser shows an infobar and no longer auto-updates), an `IndexedDB`/`Cache Storage` migration on first launch succeeds without data loss, embedded third-party SDKs that sniff `navigator.userAgentData.platform === "macOS"` and call a Monterey-removed API throw a typed `TypeError` rather than silently skipping (control), and a macOS 13+ control updates normally and retains API parity. GameZipper hosts targeting macOS 12 are moved to "deferred security update" mode and must rebuild their update channel before November 2026 to avoid the next major blocking CVE. Source: Chrome 151 release notes and Edge 151 macOS-flooring announcement, https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151 and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-relnote-stable-channel.

### Web Platform / Browser (W)

- [W-408] **[P1] Firefox 154 `RTCIceTransport.getSelectedCandidatePair()` Surfaces the Current Selected Candidate Pair and Fires `selectedcandidatepairchange`** — Firefox 154 (ships Aug 18 2026) ships `RTCIceTransport.getSelectedCandidatePair()` plus the `selectedcandidatepairchange` event (bug 2019332). For a controlled `RTCPeerConnection` that completes ICE and either later switches candidates, the page + a dedicated worker that imports the same peer object must observe `transport.getSelectedCandidatePair()` returning a non-null `RTCIceCandidatePair` whose `local` / `remote` candidates match the current `selectedCandidatePairId` from `getStats()`, the `selectedcandidatepairchange` event fires exactly once per switch, and the pre-switch handler payload is replaced with the new pair. Also verify a not-yet-connected transport returns `null` instead of throwing, and Chromium / WebKit controls retain their prior API surface without regressing or matching this exact event name. Source: Firefox 154 Beta developer release notes, https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Firefox/Releases/154 and https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=2019332.

- [W-409] **[P1] Safari 26.6 `compileStreaming` and `instantiateStreaming` Accept the `compileOptions` Parameter for JS String Builtins** — Safari 26.6 (Jul 27 2026) extends the WebAssembly JS String Builtins support to the streaming variants: `WebAssembly.compileStreaming()` and `WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming()` now accept a `compileOptions` object containing `builtins: ["js-string"]`. For a controlled `example.wasm` fixture that imports `js-string` builtins, assert the streaming control compiles/instantiates successfully (no `LinkError`), the imported builtins are callable from imported JS strings (`String.fromCharCode`, `String.fromCodePoint`), the transfer bytes equal the response body, and a control using the non-streaming `compile` + `instantiate` path produces identical exported function behavior. The previous Safari 26.2 surface that rejected a `compileOptions` argument on the streaming variants must now succeed; runtime impersonation must not silently fall back. Source: WebKit Features for Safari 26.6, Jul 27 2026, https://webkit.org/blog/18178/webkit-features-for-safari-26-6/.

### Browser Compatibility / CSS (B)

- [B-231] **[P1] Safari 26.6 `ic` Length Unit Respects Page Zoom and Equals One `em` Per CSS Spec** — Safari 26.6 fixes WebKit bug 174857144: the CSS `ic` unit (ideographic advance) was scaling incorrectly with page zoom, so it no longer equaled `1em`. After page zoom steps of 80%, 100%, 120%, 150%, and 200%, assert `getComputedStyle(el).width === "10em"` is byte-equal to `getComputedStyle(el).width === "10ic"` for any CJK glyph fixture on the test page, that the chosen-element font (e.g. Chinese) is correctly attributed to the ic-unit text, and that a control engine (Chromium or Firefox) matches the same fixture at the same zoom levels. Reject any layout result where `ic` differs from `em` at any non-100% zoom level on Safari 26.6 and onward. Source: WebKit Features for Safari 26.6, CSS `ic`/zoom fix, Jul 27 2026, https://webkit.org/blog/18178/webkit-features-for-safari-26-6/ and https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=174857144.

- [B-232] **[P1] Safari 26.6 `position-area` Falls Back When the Body Is the Only Scrollable Container** — Safari 26.6 fixes WebKit bug 175544079: a `position: fixed` element using `position-area` failed to fall back properly when the document `body` was the only scrollable container, leaving the element anchored at an unexpected coordinate or hidden behind scrollbars. For a controlled page whose `body` is the sole scrollable container and a fixed anchor toolbar that uses `position-area: top` plus `position-anchor: --gh`, set a 600px tall scroll buffer, dispatch `scrollTo(0, 200)`, and assert the toolbar's `boundingClientRect.top` matches the expected fallback value within one device pixel. Verify the toolbar remains reachable at every scroll position, no clipping by sticky/fixed layers occurs, and Safari 25 (control) reproduces the bug while Chrome / Firefox engines already pass. Source: WebKit Features for Safari 26.6, `position-area` fallback fix, Jul 27 2026, https://webkit.org/blog/18178/webkit-features-for-safari-26-6/ and https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=175544079.

### Security / Worker Delivery (S)

- [S-469] **[P1] Safari 26.6 `WKHTTPCookieStore` Can Delete Partitioned Third-Party Cookies** — Safari 26.6 fixes WebKit bug 176097960: `WKHTTPCookieStore.deleteCookie()` failed to delete partitioned (`SameSite=None; Secure; Partitioned`) third-party cookies set in the WKWebView's storage. For an in-app WebView loading a controlled third-party iframe in a partition, assert the partition's partition key is exposed to the WKWebView host, the iframe sets a partitioned cookie, `deleteCookie` (both by name and by `NSHTTPCookie`) removes it, a subsequent fetch in the partition returns no `Set-Cookie` residue, the cookie is gone from `WKHTTPCookieStore.getAllCookies()` for that partition, and a non-partitioned cookie with the same name in the parent origin remains intact. Also confirm the deletion does not affect unrelated partitions. Source: WebKit Features for Safari 26.6, partitioned-cookie deletion fix, Jul 27 2026, https://webkit.org/blog/18178/webkit-features-for-safari-26-6/ and https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=176097960.

- [S-470] **[P1] Safari 26.6 Automatically Unregisters Service Workers with Missing Main or Imported Scripts** — Safari 26.6 fixes WebKit bugs 175522651 + 175522816: service worker registrations whose main script (or any imported script) was missing stayed in the registration table, blocking re-registration with a new URL. For a controlled origin, register a service worker, replace the script file with an HTTP 404, restart Safari (or call `update()`), then attempt to register a new SW at a different URL; assert the broken registration is gone from `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistrations()`, the new SW reaches `activated`, no orphan script-fetch entry remains, and a Chromium / Firefox control where the same broken SW remains in the table (by current behavior) provides a contrast. Independently assert that installed extensions whose service worker database files accumulated across launches (bug 175810627) now have bounded size after a refresh. Source: WebKit Features for Safari 26.6, service-worker re-registration fixes, Jul 27 2026, https://webkit.org/blog/18178/webkit-features-for-safari-26-6/ and https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=175522651 and https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=175522816.

### Compatibility / Embedded Runtime (C)

- [C-224] **[P1] Safari 26.6 `RTCPeerConnection` `iceTransportPolicy: "relay"` Gathers TURN-Relay ICE Candidates on macOS Sequoia** — Safari 26.6 fixes WebKit bug 175009190: an `RTCPeerConnection` configured with `iceTransportPolicy: "relay"` failed to gather any ICE candidates on macOS Sequoia, silently breaking forced-TURN WebRTC topologies. For a controlled WebRTC handshake on a macOS Sequoia fixture with one TURN-only reachable relay, call `createOffer`, `setLocalDescription`, listen for `icecandidate`, and assert at least one relay candidate is gathered within 3 s with a non-zero `port`, `candidate` string starts with `relay`/`typ relay`, `icecandidateerror` is not emitted, `connectionState` advances toward `connected`, and a Chromium / Firefox control produces at least one matching relay candidate. Assert that `iceTransportPolicy: "all"` still gathers host and srflx candidates unchanged. Source: WebKit Features for Safari 26.6, iceTransportPolicy relay fix, Jul 27 2026, https://webkit.org/blog/18178/webkit-features-for-safari-26-6/ and https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=175009190.

### Gameplay / Display (G)

- [G-198] **[P1] Safari 26.6 iPad Desktop-Mode CSS `zoom` Correctly Binds to `font-size`, `font-weight`, `font-variant`, and `font-style`** — Safari 26.6 fixes WebKit bug 176647969: when an iPad requested the desktop website and CSS `zoom` was applied to an ancestor, `font-size`, `font-weight`, `font-variant`, and `font-style` were applied incorrectly because the rendering engine failed to interpret `zoom` as a font-binding parent. For a controlled iPad-as-desktop page with `body { zoom: 1.5 }` and inline `<span>` controls that set all four font attributes, assert `getComputedStyle(el).fontSize` is exactly `1.5 ×` the unzoomed base size (within 0.5 px), `fontWeight` / `fontStyle` / `fontVariant` round-trip identically, the rendered bitmap hash of the zoomed element matches a Chromium control at the same `zoom` level (or Firefox's equivalent `transform: scale()` fallback), and Safari 25 (control) reproduces the bug. Source: WebKit Features for Safari 26.6, iPad desktop-zoom font binding fix, Jul 27 2026, https://webkit.org/blog/18178/webkit-features-for-safari-26-6/ and https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=176647969.

*(9 new test cases this cycle. Every acceptance criterion is executable through controlled WebRTC fixtures, deterministic CSSOM computed-style reads at multi-level zoom, controlled service-worker registration/HTTP fixtures, WebKit bug reference parity, or pixel-hash and update-database size assertions; no case depends on subjective visual scoring.)*

### Security / Worker Delivery (S)

- [S-468] **[P1] Edge 150 Strict Worker MIME Checking Rejects Legacy Script Types by Default** — Serve byte-identical dedicated-worker fixtures as `text/javascript` and `application/javascript` controls, and as `text/ascii`, `text/plain`, `text/html`, or a missing `Content-Type`. In Edge 150 with `StrictMimetypeCheckForWorkerScriptsEnabled` enabled or unset, assert both JavaScript MIME controls execute once while every legacy/non-JavaScript response raises exactly one worker error, never posts a ready message, and never mutates the page sentinel; with the policy disabled in an isolated managed-profile compatibility control, assert the documented legacy `text/ascii` fixture can load while the standards-compliant controls still pass. Source: Microsoft Edge 150 stable release notes and policy documentation, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-relnote-stable-channel and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-policies/strictmimetypecheckforworkerscriptsenabled.

### Web Platform / Browser / Extension APIs (W)

- [W-403] **[P1] Firefox 153 Content Scripts Safely Read and Mutate Document and Shadow-Root Adopted Stylesheets** — In a signed fixture extension, inject a content script into a page whose `Array.prototype.push` is deliberately poisoned, create two constructed `CSSStyleSheet` objects, and assign/push them through both `document.adoptedStyleSheets` and a fixture `ShadowRoot.adoptedStyleSheets` without `.wrappedJSObject`. Assert the arrays are iterable, page and content script observe the same sheet identities and order, computed styles change in both roots and persist after a microtask, invalid non-`CSSStyleSheet` assignment throws `TypeError`, the poisoned page prototype is never invoked, and no opaque-wrapper or Xray exception occurs. Source: Firefox 153 developer release notes and Mozilla bug 1751346, https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Firefox/Releases/153 and https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=1751346.

- [W-404] **[P2] Firefox 153 WebExtension Theme Gradients Preserve Layer Order, Validation, and Background Size** — Apply a test theme whose `images.additional_backgrounds` interleaves two packaged SVGs with `{"linear-gradient":"to bottom, #FF6BBA -20%, #FFC999 50%"}`, plus matching alignment, tiling, and `properties.additional_backgrounds_size: ["auto", "auto", "auto 144px"]`. Assert `browser.theme.update()` resolves, `theme.onUpdated` fires once, `theme.getCurrent()` round-trips the three effective layers and size values, deterministic browser-chrome pixels show the first item on top and the gradient tiled at 144 px height, and network logs contain no request derived from the gradient string. Also assert an injected gradient such as `red), url(https://attacker.invalid/x), linear-gradient(transparent,` is rejected and sends zero requests. Source: Firefox 153 theme documentation and Mozilla bug 2036647, https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Add-ons/WebExtensions/manifest.json/theme and https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=2036647.

- [W-405] **[P2] Firefox 153 Contextual-Identity Capability APIs Eliminate Hard-Coded Container Palettes** — In an extension with contextual-identities permission, await `getSupportedColors()` and `getSupportedIcons()` and assert non-empty duplicate-free arrays whose entries have valid `color` plus `#RRGGBB` `colorCode`, and `icon` plus `resource://` SVG `iconUrl`. Create then remove one temporary identity for every returned color with a returned icon, and for every returned icon with a returned color; assert each created identity echoes values present in the capability arrays and every icon URL loads as non-empty SVG. When contextual identities are disabled, assert both promises reject rather than returning an unsafe partial list. Source: Firefox 153 developer release notes and MDN WebExtension API, https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Firefox/Releases/153 and https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Add-ons/WebExtensions/API/contextualIdentities/getSupportedColors.

- [W-406] **[P2] Firefox 153 Legacy Container Colors Canonicalize to `cyan` and `gray` Without Profile Breakage** — Create fixture identities using the accepted legacy colors `turquoise` and `toolbar`, plus the new `violet`; assert immediate and post-restart API readback resolves the aliases to `cyan` and `gray`, retains `violet`, and `getSupportedColors()` contains `cyan`/`gray`/`violet` but not the two legacy names. Open one tab in each identity, verify its `cookieStoreId` remains stable across restart and its storage is isolated from the other identities, then remove all fixtures successfully. Source: Firefox 153 contextual-identities documentation and Mozilla bug 2044354, https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Add-ons/WebExtensions/API/contextualIdentities/getSupportedColors and https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=2044354.

- [W-407] **[P2] Firefox 153 Local Mode Maps Multiple Fixture Directories to Isolated Custom Origins** — In a disposable Firefox 153 Developer Tools profile, use the documented Local Mode settings UI to map two controlled local directories to two distinct custom origins, then load a page, ES module, dedicated worker, IndexedDB store, service worker, and secure-context-only API probe from each mapping. Assert every realm reports the assigned origin rather than `file://`, same-origin module/worker loads succeed, storage and service-worker scopes remain isolated between mappings, unmapped paths and a percent-encoded traversal probe cannot expose either sibling directory, and deleting a mapping makes its origin unreachable without affecting the other. Source: Firefox 153 release notes, https://www.mozilla.org/en-US/firefox/153.0/releasenotes/.

### Browser Compatibility / CSS (B)

- [B-230] **[P2] Firefox 153 `alignment-baseline` Keywords Keep Multiscript SVG Game Glyphs Anchored** — Render fixed-font inline-SVG score labels containing Latin, CJK, and mathematical glyph fixtures with `alphabetic`, `ideographic`, `central`, `mathematical`, and `hanging`. Assert `CSS.supports()` is true for every keyword, each computed value matches its declaration, and the bounding-box offset from a versioned baseline path stays within one device pixel at device scale factors 1 and 2; compare against a `baseline` control and require unsupported engines to retain a readable fallback with no clipping or overlap. Source: Firefox 153 release notes and MDN CSS reference, https://www.mozilla.org/en-US/firefox/153.0/releasenotes/ and https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/alignment-baseline.

### Compatibility / Embedded Runtime (C)

- [C-223] **[P2] Edge 150 WebView2 Per-Application Downgrade Selects Only an Installed Major and Fails Back Safely** — On an enterprise-managed Windows fixture with current, N-1, and N-2 WebView2 runtimes installed side-by-side, set `DowngradeVersion` by executable name for two minimal host apps and assert each `BrowserVersionString` matches its digits-only requested major while an unlisted control uses Evergreen. Test a full-version value, wildcard, non-digit value, and absent major; assert each has no policy effect and falls back to `BrowserExecutableFolder` or Evergreen rather than launching the wrong runtime. Advance the simulated release once and assert the former N-1 target remains installed as N-2 while the former N-2 app returns to current Evergreen, matching the documented auto-expiry lifecycle. Source: Microsoft Edge 150 stable and WebView2 policy documentation, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-relnote-stable-channel and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-webview-policies#downgradeversion.
