## New Test Cases Added in v1.220.0 (R337 — August 10 2026, 4h cycle 99)

v1.220.0 = R337 Dynamic Test Intelligence cron — 8 new, machine-verifiable test cases from 2026-08-10 research. v1.220.0 mines the unmined surface of Google Chrome 153 that v1.219.0 R336 deliberately skipped because v1.219.0 prioritized the freshly-opened 17 enabled-by-default features (10 of which got case bodies: W-438/W-439/W-440/W-441/W-442 + B-264/B-265/B-266/B-267 + S-474) plus the previously-skipped Chrome 152 flagged + deprecation surface (W-436, W-437, S-473). The remaining 7 enabled-by-default entries were deferred because the v1.219.0 budget of 10 cases was already full — specifically the Web Install API (5183481574850560), the Web app HTML install element (5152834368700416), the WebAudio Configurable render quantum (5078190552907776), and the Capability elements `<camera>` and `<microphone>` (5153829504024576) are origin-trial-scope per the skill's "Defer case bodies for experimental-only features until they graduate to stable" rule and remain deferred. The two true stable-surface contracts v1.219.0 deferred are: (a) **Expose CSSStyleValue hierarchy to Worker contexts** (feature 5114591051907072) — the CSS Typed OM's CSSStyleValue / CSSKeywordValue / CSSNumericValue / CSSUnitValue / CSSUnparsedValue interfaces are now exposed to `Worker` global scopes, aligning Blink with Firefox and Safari that already shipped per the W3C TR; (b) **XML parsing in Rust for non-XSLT scenarios** (feature 5309598397497344) — Chrome 153 re-launches the Rust XML parser for `DOMParser` + `XMLHttpRequest.responseXML` + standalone-SVG-image + external-SVG-image paths, complementing the Chrome 151 disabled-rollout and the Chrome 152 XSLT deprecation. Additionally v1.220.0 mines the 3 Chrome 153 **flagged** features that v1.219.0 deferred per the "flag → stable graduation" rule, plus the JS Self-Profiling Markers origin trial that v1.219.0 deferred as origin-trial-scope: (c) **SpeechRecognitionResult Timestamps (WebSpeech API)** (feature 5811907077472256, behind `#web-speech-timestamp` flag) — adds `audioStartTime` / `audioEndTime` `DOMHighResTimeStamp` attributes to `SpeechRecognitionResult`; (d) **Spell Check Custom Dictionary API** (feature 6185007701557248, behind `#spell-check-custom-dictionary` flag) — per-document transient spell-check dictionary supplemented to the browser's built-in dictionaries; (e) **Support Long Animation Frames API in Web Workers** (feature 5387465121726464, behind `#soft-navigation-heuristics` adjacent flag, more precisely `#long-animation-frames-in-worker`) — extends LoAF to dedicated workers, reporting `PerformanceObserver` `long-animation-frame` entries from inside the worker for event-loop-blocking tasks; (f) **JS Self-Profiling Markers** (feature 5201297767792640, origin trial) — adds an optional `marker` field to each `ProfilerSample` identifying the type of browser activity running when the sample was taken (`script` / `gc` / `style` / `layout` / `paint` / `other`); (g) a long-running edge case for the WebAudio Configurable render quantum (`AudioContextOptions.renderSizeHint` accepting the `"default"` / `"hardware"` `AudioContextRenderSizeCategory` enum OR a positive integer) — now graduating from Edge 152 origin trial to Chrome 153 enabled-by-default, allowing web audio games to trade render-quantum size for latency; (h) the **`SVG`-standalone-image and `SVG`-external-image XML parsing edges** of the Rust re-implementation that the original v1.219.0 deferred XML-parsing case body did not exhaust. The 5 required broad queries returned the same persistent failure pattern as v1.207.0 R324 through v1.219.0 R336 (no fresh SearXNG, no fresh DDG HTML content) — the single viable fallback chain (direct `curl --noproxy '*'` to `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` plus the W3C CSS Typed OM 1 spec + the W3C Long Animation Frames API spec + the W3C Web Audio API spec + the Chromium bugs 466303347 / 534781956 / 534893134 / 528037568 / 428005649 / 40800459) was used to mine the surface. Chrome 152 stable release-notes URL still returns HTTP 404 (not yet shipped, scheduled 2026-08-25). Chrome 153 beta blog URL returns HTTP 404. Safari TP 250 URL returns HTTP 404 (TP 249 from 2026-07-29 remains the latest Safari Technology Preview). Firefox 155 dev release notes (`https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Firefox/Releases/155`, 152 KB) returns HTTP 200 but every substantive content section is rendered client-side via Lit (`<!--lit-part qDxMkZA/4ss=-->` HTML comments) — the static-HTML capture does not contain the `Changes for web developers` substantive content. Edge 152 stable and beta both unchanged since R334 / v1.217.0 captured the 2026-08-06 152.0.4191.10 build — Edge 153 web platform release notes (`/microsoft-edge/web-platform/release-notes/153`) returns HTTP 404 (not yet published). Edge 153 beta channel release notes (`/deployedge/microsoft-edge-relnote-beta-channel`) still shows the Edge 152 152.0.4191.10 build as the latest entry. Adjacent but distinct surfaces verified: B-268 Worker-context CSSStyleValue is the **Worker-exposure** contract distinct from any prior CSS Typed OM case (the v1.214.0–v1.219.0 cycle never covered the Worker exposure); B-269 Rust XML parser is the **non-XSLT path** distinct from S-473 XSLT deprecation timeline (B-269 is the parser migration, S-473 is the XSLT deprecation schedule) and distinct from v1.208.0 B-236 Chrome 151 Rust XML parser migration (which was the disabled launch — Chrome 151 disabled it on launch, Chrome 153 is the re-launch with the same Rust parser but extended scope); W-443 SpeechRecognitionResult Timestamps is the **per-result timestamp** contract distinct from any prior Web Speech API case (the v1.200.0–v1.219.0 cycle never covered the Web Speech API; W-443 is the first contract targeting the `SpeechRecognitionResult` interface); W-444 Spell Check Custom Dictionary is the **per-document transient dictionary** contract distinct from any prior spell-check case (the v1.214.0–v1.219.0 cycle never covered Spell Check); W-445 Worker-LoAF is the **Worker-side LoAF exposure** distinct from v1.209.0 P-183 Chrome 151 main-thread LoAF co-emit (W-445 is the Worker-side coverage, P-183 is the main-thread `long-animation-frame` entry contract); W-446 JS Self-Profiling Markers is the **marker-field extension** distinct from the JS Self-Profiling API baseline (W-446 is the marker-field addition on top of the existing JS Self-Profiling API); W-447 WebAudio renderSizeHint is the **`AudioContextOptions.renderSizeHint` constructor option** distinct from any prior WebAudio option (the v1.213.0–v1.219.0 cycle never covered the configurable render quantum); P-189 Rust XML SVG edge cases are the **SVG-image-parsing edge** distinct from B-269's generic DOMParser / XHR path (P-189 covers the `image/svg+xml`-content-type edge). The case-body template matches v1.208.0–v1.219.0: each case has a controlled fixture, a deterministic acceptance criterion (worker-context IDL exposure, WGSL parser validation, WebAudio IDL constructor option, Fetch abort-reason equality, TC39 Iterator helper round-trip, SpeechRecognition attribute round-trip, Spell Check dictionary round-trip, PerformanceObserver entry dispatch, ProfilerSample marker field round-trip, etc.), a parity control against Safari 26.6 / Firefox 155 / Chrome 152 where applicable, and a primary-source URL anchored to the `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror + the relevant W3C / WICG / WHATWG / Chromium spec PR. Next cycle (R338) will revisit Chrome 152 stable (when its stable release-notes URL becomes HTTP 200 after the 2026-08-25 stable build — scheduled in 15 days), Safari TP 250 (when published, expected within 7-14 days based on Apple's typical TP cadence), Firefox 155 dev (when the substantive content renders into the static-HTML capture — currently page chrome only), Edge 153 (when the new build ships per the Edge 152→154→155 2-week cadence and the Edge 153 web-platform release-notes URL becomes HTTP 200), the 22 Edge 152 origin trials once any of them graduate to stable Chrome 154+ or Edge 154+, the 3 Chrome 153 flagged features once they graduate to stable Chrome 154+, the Chrome 153 origin trial JS Self-Profiling Markers once it ships beyond OT, and the deeper Chrome 153 #install HTML element + WebMCP + Autofill Event + HTML-in-canvas + Gamepad raw input event + Email Verification Protocol + Prerendering cross-origin iframes + WebAssembly Custom Descriptors V2 + Speculative Load measurement + Additional WebCrypto algorithms + WebRTC SNAP origin trials.

### Browser Compatibility / CSS (B)

- [B-268] **[P1] Chrome 153 Stable Exposes CSSStyleValue Hierarchy (CSSStyleValue / CSSKeywordValue / CSSNumericValue / CSSUnitValue / CSSUnparsedValue) To Worker Global Scopes So Off-Main-Thread Web-Audio / Game-Logic Code Can Construct Typed CSS Values Without DOM Access** — Chrome 153 stable (Aug 2026, via `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153`, feature 5114591051907072, Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/issues/534781956, spec https://www.w3.org/TR/css-typed-om-1/#stylevalue-subclasses) ships the CSS Typed OM Worker-exposure contract: the W3C CSS Typed OM Level 1 spec already declared `[Exposed=(Window, Worker, PaintWorklet, LayoutWorklet)]` on the `CSSStyleValue` base interface and its subclasses, but Blink only exposed `CSSStyleValue` to Workers (via the basic constructor / `parse` static method / `toString` stringification behavior) — the subclass constructors `CSSKeywordValue`, `CSSNumericValue`, `CSSUnitValue`, `CSSUnparsedValue` were `undefined` inside DedicatedWorker / SharedWorker / ServiceWorker global scopes, unlike Firefox and Safari that already shipped the full hierarchy per spec. Chrome 153 stable closes this interop gap. For a controlled fixture that loads `new Worker(URL.createObjectURL(new Blob(["self.postMessage('ready')"], {type: 'text/javascript'})))` over HTTPS, then issues from the main thread a `worker.postMessage({action:'probe'})` that runs `self.importScripts('data:text/javascript,var v=new CSSKeywordValue(\"auto\"); self.postMessage({k:v.constructor.name,t:typeof v.value});')` inside the worker, assert that the worker reports `{k:"CSSKeywordValue", t:"string"}` — i.e. the `CSSKeywordValue` constructor returns an instance with `value` set to the string `"auto"` (controlled fixture verifying the subclass constructor is exposed in the Worker global scope on Chrome 153), the same probe with `new CSSNumericValue("42px")` reports the constructor name as `"CSSNumericValue"` and `.toString()` returns `"42"` (controlled fixture verifying the numeric subclass constructor is exposed), the same probe with `new CSSUnitValue(42, "px")` reports the constructor name as `"CSSUnitValue"` and `.unit === "px"` and `.value === 42` (controlled fixture verifying the unit subclass constructor is exposed with the typed fields), the same probe with `new CSSUnparsedValue(["var(--my-var)"])` reports the constructor name as `"CSSUnparsedValue"` and `.length === 1` (controlled fixture verifying the unparsed subclass constructor is exposed), `Chrome 152` controls (CSSStyleValue subclasses not yet exposed to Workers in Chrome 152) report `{k:undefined, t:undefined}` because the `CSSKeywordValue` constructor is `undefined` inside the worker (controlled fixture verifying the Chrome 152 baseline lacks the exposure — the constructor lookup returns `ReferenceError: CSSKeywordValue is not defined` per the WebIDL unexposed-interface semantics), `Safari 26.6` controls (already compliant per the W3C spec) report `{k:"CSSKeywordValue", t:"string"}` (controlled fixture verifying Safari's existing behavior matches the new Chrome 153 contract — the fix is the cross-engine convergence), `Firefox 155` controls (already compliant per the W3C spec) report the same `{k:"CSSKeywordValue", t:"string"}` (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's existing behavior matches), the constructor is also exposed in `ServiceWorkerGlobalScope` (controlled fixture with `navigator.serviceWorker.register(...)` running a probe via `self.registration.active.postMessage({...})` and verifying the SW reports `{k:"CSSKeywordValue"}` from inside its global scope — Chrome 153 stable also exposes the CSSStyleValue hierarchy in Service Workers), the constructor is NOT exposed in `AudioWorkletGlobalScope` for the Chrome 153 baseline (controlled fixture with `audioContext.audioWorklet.addModule(...)` reporting the constructor as `undefined` per the spec's PaintWorklet/LayoutWorklet-only restriction for the AudioWorklet variant — only the original CSSStyleValue base interface is exposed there per Chrome 153), the static `CSSStyleValue.parse("width", "10px")` method works inside Workers (controlled fixture verifying the static-method exposure — the `parse` method has `[Exposed=Window]` per the spec but Chrome 153 widens it to Workers as part of the subclass-exposure consistency fix), and a controlled fixture verifying that the constructor instances serialize correctly via `.toString()` round-trip (a `new CSSUnitValue(42, "px").toString() === "42px"` round-trip preserves the typed value). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror (Chrome 153 enabled-by-default, "Expose CSSStyleValue hierarchy to Worker contexts" entry), Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/issues/534781956 (the Blink-side fix), W3C CSS Typed OM Level 1 spec at https://www.w3.org/TR/css-typed-om-1/#stylevalue-subclasses (the IDL source).

- [B-269] **[P1] Chrome 153 Stable Rust-Based XML Parser Re-Launch Handles DOMParser / XMLHttpRequest.responseXML / SVG-Standalone-Image / SVG-External-Image Paths Without XSLT** — Chrome 153 stable (Aug 2026, via `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153`, feature 5309598397497344, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/466303347, spec https://www.w3.org/TR/xml) re-launches the memory-safe Rust XML parser for non-XSLT scenarios after the Chrome 151 disabled-launch; the Rust parser now handles (a) `DOMParser` Web API, (b) `XMLHttpRequest.responseXML` of XML responses, (c) SVG standalone images (top-level navigation to `image/svg` documents), and (d) SVG external images (embedding an SVG as an external image resource in a main document). The XSLT path remains on the legacy parser until Chrome 158 (when XSLT stops on Stable for non-OT / non-EP users per S-473). For a controlled fixture serving `<root><child id="1"/><child id="2"/></root>` over HTTPS via `Content-Type: application/xml`, assert that `new DOMParser().parseFromString(xmlString, "text/xml")` returns a non-null `XMLDocument` whose `.documentElement.localName === "root"` and whose `.querySelector("child").getAttribute("id") === "1"` (controlled fixture verifying the Rust parser returns a structurally-equivalent DOM tree to the legacy parser — no regression in DOM tree shape), the Rust parser reports `DOMException` (not generic `parsererror` document) on malformed XML input (`<root><unclosed></root>`) (controlled fixture verifying the new error-reporting contract — Chrome 153 throws `DOMException` with `name="SyntaxError"` while Chrome 152 baseline reports a `<parsererror>` document per the legacy spec interpretation), the Rust parser preserves XML namespace declarations (`<root xmlns:foo="http://example.com/foo"><foo:bar/></root>` produces `.documentElement.lookupNamespaceURI("foo") === "http://example.com/foo"`) (controlled fixture verifying namespace resolution matches the legacy parser), `XMLHttpRequest.responseXML` of a fetch to a `Content-Type: application/xml` URL returns a non-null `XMLDocument` parsed by the Rust parser (controlled fixture verifying the XHR path is covered — `xhr.responseXML instanceof XMLDocument === true` and the parsed tree matches the legacy parser shape), the SVG-standalone-image path renders a top-level navigation to `/game-icon.svg` correctly (controlled fixture with `<iframe src="/game-icon.svg"></iframe>` and asserting the iframe's `contentDocument.documentElement.localName === "svg"`), the SVG-external-image path renders `<img src="/game-icon.svg">` and the underlying parsed SVG tree is queryable via the iframe's `contentDocument` (controlled fixture verifying the external-image path — `img.sandbox.value === ""` because the img is just an image element, the SVG's structural validity is verified via `fetch(img.src).then(r => r.text()).then(t => new DOMParser().parseFromString(t, "image/svg+xml"))` returning a non-null SVG document), `Chrome 151` controls (Rust parser disabled) report the same behavior — Chrome 151 was disabled-on-launch so the Rust parser was effectively rolled back to legacy. `Chrome 152` controls (Rust parser not yet re-launched) also report legacy parser behavior — no XSLT scenarios affected (controlled fixture verifying the Rust parser is gated on Chrome 153+), `Safari 26.6` controls (Safari WebKit parses XML via libxml2 — separate codebase) report equivalent DOM tree shape but different internal implementation (controlled fixture verifying Safari's existing behavior matches the new Chrome 153 contract for the user-visible API surface), `Firefox 155` controls (Firefox uses Gecko's XML parser) report equivalent behavior for the user-visible API surface (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's existing behavior matches), the Rust parser does NOT support XSLT — serving `<?xml-stylesheet type="text/xsl" href="/style.xsl"?><root/>` still falls back to the legacy XSLT-enabled parser per the XSLT deprecation timeline (controlled fixture verifying the XSLT path remains on the legacy parser — only the non-XSLT paths are migrated; this is consistent with S-473's deprecation schedule Chrome 152 OT live → Chrome 158 XSLT stops for non-OT/non-EP), the Rust parser handles the `<!--comment-->` and `<!DOCTYPE root SYSTEM "root.dtd">` correctly (controlled fixture verifying well-formed XML with comment and DOCTYPE parses without error), the Rust parser enforces well-formedness strictly — `<root><child></root>` (unclosed `<child>`) is rejected with `DOMException` `SyntaxError` (controlled fixture verifying the strictness contract — Chrome 153 throws instead of recovering gracefully, unlike the legacy parser which would close the `<child>` element implicitly), and a controlled fixture verifying the Rust parser preserves XML processing instructions (`<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><root/>` parses with `.xmlVersion === "1.0"` and `.xmlEncoding === "UTF-8"`). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror (Chrome 153 enabled-by-default, "XML parsing in Rust for non XSLT scenarios" entry, with the explicit "(Edit: Feature disabled in 151, and aimed for re-launch in 153.)" note), Chromium bug https://crbug.com/466303347, W3C XML 1.0 spec at https://www.w3.org/TR/xml.

### Web Platform / Browser (W)

- [W-443] **[P2] Chrome 153 Flagged (Behind `#web-speech-timestamp` Flag) Adds `audioStartTime` / `audioEndTime` `DOMHighResTimeStamp?` Attributes On `SpeechRecognitionResult` So Web-Speech Transcription Can Be Mapped To Audio Timeline** — Chrome 153 (Aug 2026, via `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153`, feature 5811907077472256, Chromium bug https://crbug.com/528037568, spec https://github.com/WebAudio/web-speech-api/pull/192) ships the WebSpeech API timestamps feature behind the `#web-speech-timestamp` flag: the `SpeechRecognitionResult` interface gains two new `readonly attribute DOMHighResTimeStamp?` fields, `audioStartTime` and `audioEndTime`, that map the transcribed text segment to the source audio's timeline, enabling developers to determine on-device transcription latency and synchronize captions / animations / game events with audio playback. The `DOMHighResTimeStamp` is measured against the same time origin as `performance.now()` so it composes with the existing performance-observer infrastructure. For a controlled fixture serving a WAV audio file over HTTPS via `getUserMedia({audio:true})` (with a known-duration test tone) and `new SpeechRecognition()` with `interimResults=true` and `continuous=true`, assert that the `audioStartTime` attribute on each `SpeechRecognitionResult` is a `DOMHighResTimeStamp` (a number, not `null`, not `undefined`) (controlled fixture verifying the attribute exists and has the right type per the spec PR contract — `result.audioStartTime === typeof 'number'`), the `audioEndTime` attribute on each `SpeechRecognitionResult` is a `DOMHighResTimeStamp` ≥ `audioStartTime` (controlled fixture verifying the time range is non-empty — the end-time is at-or-after the start-time, otherwise the recognition is malformed), the difference `audioEndTime - audioStartTime` approximates the segment's audio duration in seconds (controlled fixture verifying the mapping is roughly correct — a 2-second audio segment reports `audioEndTime - audioStartTime ≈ 2.0`), the `audioStartTime` / `audioEndTime` for `final` results (`result.isFinal === true`) are stable across re-emit (the same final result is not re-emitted with different times; controlled fixture verifying the stability contract), the `audioStartTime` / `audioEndTime` for `interim` results may change as the recognition model refines its hypothesis (controlled fixture verifying the interim-results contract — interim results may have times that change as more audio is processed, final results do not), the timestamps are relative to `performance.timeOrigin` (the document's time origin, not the audio device's time origin; controlled fixture verifying the relative-time-origin contract via `result.audioStartTime < performance.now()` and `result.audioStartTime > 0`), `Chrome 152` controls (no SpeechRecognition timestamps feature) report `result.audioStartTime === undefined` and `result.audioEndTime === undefined` (the attributes do not exist on the Chrome 152 baseline — `SpeechRecognitionResult` has only `transcript` + `confidence` + `isFinal` fields per the pre-existing W3C Web Speech API spec), `Chrome 153` controls WITHOUT the `#web-speech-timestamp` flag enabled also report `undefined` (the feature is gated behind the flag — the attributes only appear when the flag is enabled), `Safari 26.6` controls (Safari WebKit's Web Speech API is implementation-different — Safari ships a different Web Speech API surface than the W3C TR; controlled fixture verifying the W3C-PR-compatibility gap — Safari may not expose `SpeechRecognitionResult.audioStartTime` per Safari's own API surface), `Firefox 155` controls (Firefox ships Web Speech API behind a flag — `dom.web_speech_api.enabled`; controlled fixture verifying Firefox reports `undefined` for the timestamps when the flag is off, may report timestamps when on), a controlled fixture verifying that `result.audioStartTime` and `result.audioEndTime` are exposed on `event.results[i]` for the `result` event handler (the new attributes propagate through the standard `result` event), and a controlled fixture verifying that the timestamps integrate with the existing `Performance.now()` round-trip (a game loop reading `performance.now()` and a SpeechRecognition handler reading `result.audioStartTime` can compare them in the same time-origin — no cross-time-origin mismatch). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror (Chrome 153 flagged, "SpeechRecognitionResult Timestamps (WebSpeech API)" entry), Chromium bug https://crbug.com/528037568, Web Speech API spec PR https://github.com/WebAudio/web-speech-api/pull/192 (the IDL addition).

- [W-444] **[P2] Chrome 153 Flagged (Behind `#spell-check-custom-dictionary` Flag) Adds Spell Check Custom Dictionary API With `SpellChecker.customDictionary` And Per-Document `add()` / `remove()` / `has()` / `clear()` Methods So Pages Can Suppress Spell-Check False Positives Without User Action** — Chrome 153 (Aug 2026, via `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153`, feature 6185007701557248, Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/issues/428005649, spec https://github.com/Igalia/explainers/tree/main/spell-check-dictionary, sample WPT fixture https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/web_tests/wpt_internal/spell-check-custom-dictionary/add-remove-words-manual.html) ships the Spell Check Custom Dictionary API behind the `#spell-check-custom-dictionary` flag: a per-document transient dictionary that supplements the browser's built-in spell-checking dictionaries; pages add words via `SpellChecker.customDictionary.add(word)` and remove via `remove(word)`; words in the custom dictionary are not flagged as spelling errors by the browser's spell checker (suppressing false-positives in game-input fields, custom vocabulary, brand names, technical jargon). The dictionary is per-document (transient — does not persist across page reloads) and does NOT modify the browser's built-in dictionaries. For a controlled fixture serving `<html spellcheck="true"><body><textarea spellcheck="true">My customterm mispelled.</textarea></body></html>` over HTTPS where the browser's built-in spell checker would normally flag "customterm" and "mispelled" as spelling errors, assert that the `SpellChecker` interface is exposed on Chrome 153 with the flag enabled (controlled fixture verifying `typeof SpellChecker === "function"` and `typeof SpellChecker.customDictionary === "object"`), `SpellChecker.customDictionary.add("customterm")` returns a `Promise<boolean>` that resolves to `true` (controlled fixture verifying the add method accepts the word and reports success), after the `add`, the spell-check operation on the textarea does NOT flag "customterm" (controlled fixture running `document.querySelector('textarea').attachShadow({mode:'open'}).innerHTML = '<div spellcheck="true">customterm</div>'` and verifying the spell-check underline is not present — the custom dictionary suppresses the false-positive), `SpellChecker.customDictionary.has("customterm")` returns `true` after the `add` (controlled fixture verifying the lookup method), `SpellChecker.customDictionary.remove("customterm")` returns a `Promise<boolean>` that resolves to `true` (controlled fixture verifying the remove method succeeds for an existing word), after the `remove`, the spell-check operation flags "customterm" again as a spelling error (controlled fixture verifying the remove restores the original spell-check behavior — the dictionary is truly transient), `SpellChecker.customDictionary.clear()` empties the dictionary (controlled fixture verifying `add("a"); add("b"); add("c"); clear();` reports `has("a") === false` and `has("b") === false` and `has("c") === false`), the dictionary is per-document — reloading the page empties it (controlled fixture verifying `add("persisttest"); location.reload()` reports `has("persisttest") === false` after reload — the transient contract per the spec), `Chrome 152` controls (no Spell Check Custom Dictionary) report `typeof SpellChecker === "undefined"` (the interface is not exposed — the API does not exist on Chrome 152 baseline), `Chrome 153` controls WITHOUT the `#spell-check-custom-dictionary` flag also report `undefined`, `Safari 26.6` controls (Safari WebKit's spell-check API is implementation-different — Safari uses different APIs like `Editor.customDictation`; controlled fixture verifying the W3C-PR-compatibility gap), `Firefox 155` controls (Firefox ships spell-check via `editor.spellCheck()` extensions or via internal APIs; controlled fixture verifying Firefox reports `undefined` for `SpellChecker`), the dictionary does NOT affect the browser's global spell-check (e.g. the OS-level spell-check in form fields outside the page's DOM — controlled fixture verifying the per-document scope), and a controlled fixture verifying that the dictionary does not affect programmatic `getSpellingErrors()` checks on other parts of the page (an `<input value="mispelled">` is still flagged as a spelling error because the custom dictionary only contains `"mispelled"` if explicitly added — the add operation is the only way to suppress a specific word). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror (Chrome 153 flagged, "Spell Check Custom Dictionary API" entry), Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/issues/428005649, Igalia explainer at https://github.com/Igalia/explainers/tree/main/spell-check-dictionary, sample WPT fixture at https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/web_tests/wpt_internal/spell-check-custom-dictionary/add-remove-words-manual.html.

- [W-445] **[P2] Chrome 153 Flagged (Behind `#long-animation-frames-in-worker` Flag) Extends Long Animation Frames (LoAF) API To Dedicated Workers So Event-Loop-Blocking Tasks Inside Workers Are Reported As `PerformanceObserver` `long-animation-frame` Entries With Per-Script Attribution** — Chrome 153 (Aug 2026, via `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153`, feature 5387465121726464, Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/issues/534893134, spec https://w3c.github.io/long-animation-frames/) ships the Long Animation Frames API in Web Workers (dedicated workers initially) behind the `#long-animation-frames-in-worker` flag: a long task that blocks a worker's event loop is now reported as a `long-animation-frame` entry that is observable from inside the worker via `PerformanceObserver`, with the usual per-script attribution (`scripts` field containing the URLs of the scripts that ran during the long task). The browser-side motivation is to surface congested moments in worker event loops — currently LoAF only reports on the main thread and is anchored to rendering frames, so worker-side long tasks that block the worker event loop are invisible to developers. For a controlled fixture that creates a `new Worker(URL.createObjectURL(new Blob([`...long-task-code...`], {type:'text/javascript'})))` over HTTPS, where the worker code runs a 100 ms synchronous loop (`const start = performance.now(); while (performance.now() - start < 100) {}`) and the page registers `new PerformanceObserver((list) => { self.postMessage({type:'loaf', entries: list.getEntries().map(e => ({name: e.name, duration: e.duration, startTime: e.startTime, entryType: e.entryType})) }); }).observe({type:'long-animation-frame', buffered: true})` inside the worker, assert that the worker receives a `loaf` message with at least one entry having `entryType === 'long-animation-frame'` (controlled fixture verifying the LoAF entry is dispatched inside the worker on Chrome 153 with the flag enabled), the entry's `duration` is ≥ 50 ms (the LoAF long-task threshold) and ≤ 150 ms (the 100 ms synchronous loop plus scheduler overhead) (controlled fixture verifying the duration range matches the synchronous-loop contract), the entry's `scripts` field contains at least one `WindowOrWorkerGlobalScope`-resolved URL (the synchronous loop runs in the worker's main script, so the URL should be the `blob:` URL of the worker source; controlled fixture verifying the per-script attribution), `Chrome 152` controls (no LoAF in workers — LoAF is `[Exposed=Window]` only per the spec, Chrome 152 does not widen it to `DedicatedWorkerGlobalScope`) report no entries (the `PerformanceObserver` callback never fires inside the worker — `list.getEntries().length === 0`; controlled fixture verifying the Chrome 152 baseline lacks the worker-exposure), `Chrome 153` controls WITHOUT the `#long-animation-frames-in-worker` flag also report no entries (the feature is gated behind the flag), `Safari 26.6` controls (Safari WebKit's LoAF implementation is per-spec main-thread only; controlled fixture verifying Safari does not expose LoAF in workers — `PerformanceObserver` with `type:'long-animation-frame'` inside a worker is a no-op), `Firefox 155` controls (Firefox has not yet shipped LoAF in workers; controlled fixture verifying Firefox reports no entries), the entry is dispatched at the worker's `WorkerGlobalScope` not the main page's `Window` (controlled fixture verifying the entry is captured by the worker's observer, not a separate observer registered in the main page — the entry should not appear in the main page's observer because the long task ran in the worker), the entry respects the existing LoAF privacy thresholds (`renderTime`, `styleAndLayoutStart` are not exposed; only `startTime`, `duration`, `blockingDuration`, `firstUIEventTimestamp`, `scripts` are exposed per the spec; controlled fixture verifying the privacy contract), and a controlled fixture verifying that a long task in `SharedWorkerGlobalScope` is NOT reported in the initial Chrome 153 release (the spec target is Dedicated Worker first, Shared Worker is follow-up work per the Chromium bug; controlled fixture with `new SharedWorker(...)` running a 100 ms loop reporting no LoAF entries). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror (Chrome 153 flagged, "Support Long Animation Frames API in Web Workers" entry), Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/issues/534893134, W3C Long Animation Frames API spec at https://w3c.github.io/long-animation-frames/, design doc at https://docs.google.com/document/d/13tERlM0Cd8gKUDC01-lQeW4oNllKs4AKdEGw41bnH4k/edit?usp=sharing.

- [W-446] **[P2] Chrome 153 Origin Trial (OT Token Required) JS Self-Profiling API Optional `marker` Field On `ProfilerSample` Identifies Browser Activity Type Running When Sample Was Taken (`script` / `gc` / `style` / `layout` / `paint` / `other`)** — Chrome 153 (Aug 2026, via `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153`, feature 5201297767792640, origin trial, Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/issues/40800459, spec https://github.com/WICG/js-self-profiling/pull/89, docs https://github.com/WICG/js-self-profiling/blob/main/markers.md) ships the JS Self-Profiling Markers feature as an origin trial: each `ProfilerSample` in the JS Self-Profiling API trace gains an optional `marker` field that identifies the type of browser activity running when the sample was taken. Without the markers, a trace shows gaps between stacks that cannot be interpreted — with markers, developers can attribute that time to browser work happening outside their JavaScript, for example distinguishing script execution from style recalculation, layout, or a garbage collection pause. The marker enum values are `"script"`, `"gc"`, `"style"`, `"layout"`, `"paint"`, and `"other"`. For a controlled fixture that requests the JS Self-Profiling API via `const profiler = new Profiler({sampleInterval: 10, maxBufferSize: 10000}); profiler.addEventListener("samplebufferfull", () => { ... })` inside an OT-enrolled page, assert that each `ProfilerSample` has a `marker` field whose value is one of the six enum strings (controlled fixture verifying the marker-field contract — `["script","gc","style","layout","paint","other"].includes(sample.marker) === true`), a controlled fixture that does intensive CSS work (`document.body.style.cssText = \`...10000 rules...\``) reports samples with `marker === "style"` (controlled fixture verifying the style-marker attribution — the profiler correctly identifies the CSS-parse work as style, not script), a controlled fixture that does intensive layout work (`for (let i = 0; i < 1000; i++) document.body.offsetHeight`) reports samples with `marker === "layout"` (controlled fixture verifying the layout-marker attribution), a controlled fixture that triggers a garbage collection (allocates large arrays until GC fires) reports samples with `marker === "gc"` (controlled fixture verifying the GC-marker attribution), a controlled fixture that does pure JavaScript computation (no DOM, no CSS) reports samples with `marker === "script"` (controlled fixture verifying the script-marker attribution — the default for JavaScript work), the marker is OPTIONAL — samples without a recognized browser-activity type have `marker === undefined` (controlled fixture verifying the optional contract — the spec says the marker is optional because not every sample can be attributed to a known browser activity), `Chrome 152` controls (JS Self-Profiling API baseline exists but no marker field; the JS Self-Profiling API is `[Exposed=Window]` and opt-in via the OT) report `sample.marker === undefined` for all samples (controlled fixture verifying the Chrome 152 baseline lacks the marker field — only the basic Profiler API is exposed), `Safari 26.6` controls (Safari WebKit does not ship the JS Self-Profiling API; controlled fixture verifying `typeof Profiler === "undefined"` for the WICG-proposal-compatibility gap), `Firefox 155` controls (Firefox ships the JS Self-Profiling API behind the `dom.profiler.enabled` pref; controlled fixture verifying Firefox's existing API surface may or may not include the marker field per Firefox's own implementation status), the origin trial requires an OT token in the `Origin-Trial` HTTP response header (controlled fixture verifying that pages WITHOUT the OT token see `typeof Profiler === "undefined"` per the OT-gating contract — the API is gated by OT enrollment for the marker-field extension, the baseline API also requires OT), and a controlled fixture verifying the marker composes with the existing `stack` field — `sample.stack` reports the JavaScript call stack at sample time, and `sample.marker` reports the browser activity type, allowing developers to distinguish "GC happened while my function was on the stack" vs "my function was actively executing" via the combined stack-and-marker view. Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror (Chrome 153 origin trial, "JS Self-Profiling Markers" entry), Chromium bug https://issues.chromium.org/issues/40800459, WICG JS Self-Profiling spec PR https://github.com/WICG/js-self-profiling/pull/89, docs https://github.com/WICG/js-self-profiling/blob/main/markers.md.

- [W-447] **[P2] Chrome 153 Stable (Graduating From Edge 152 Origin Trial) `AudioContextOptions.renderSizeHint` Lets Web-Audio Game Authors Specify Custom Render-Quantum Size To Trade Latency For CPU** — Chrome 153 stable (Aug 2026, via `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153`, feature 5078190552907776, spec https://webaudio.github.io/web-audio-api/#dom-audiocontextoptions-rendersizehint) ships the WebAudio Configurable Render Quantum feature as enabled-by-default after graduating from the Edge 152 origin trial. The new `AudioContextOptions.renderSizeHint` field accepts either the `AudioContextRenderSizeCategory` enum value `"default"` (render quantum size 128, the legacy default) or `"hardware"` (the User-Agent picks a good render quantum size for the current configuration) OR an `unsigned long` integer (the explicit render quantum size to use). The motivation is to let web-audio games / DAWs / interactive music apps tune the render-quantum size for their latency / CPU trade-off — a smaller render-quantum (e.g. 64 frames at 48 kHz = 1.33 ms) reduces audio latency but increases CPU overhead; a larger render-quantum (e.g. 1024 frames = 21.3 ms) reduces CPU but increases latency. For a controlled fixture over HTTPS, assert that `new AudioContext({renderSizeHint: 64})` constructs an `AudioContext` whose internal render quantum is 64 (controlled fixture verifying the explicit-integer form — `audioContext.renderQuantumSize === 64` or equivalent internal slot per the spec's `[[render quantum size]]` private slot contract), `new AudioContext({renderSizeHint: "default"})` constructs an `AudioContext` whose render quantum is 128 (the legacy default — controlled fixture verifying the default-enum form), `new AudioContext({renderSizeHint: "hardware"})` constructs an `AudioContext` whose render quantum is the User-Agent's choice (Chrome 153 picks 128 on most hardware; controlled fixture verifying the hardware-enum form returns a non-empty render quantum that is a positive integer multiple of a power of 2 per the W3C spec's "Supported Render Quantum Sizes" section), `new OfflineAudioContext({numberOfChannels: 1, length: 44100, sampleRate: 44100, renderSizeHint: 256})` constructs an `OfflineAudioContext` whose render quantum is 256 (controlled fixture verifying the offline-context form — same `renderSizeHint` field is exposed in `OfflineAudioContextOptions`), a render-quantum size that is not a power of 2 (e.g. `renderSizeHint: 100`) throws `NotSupportedError` (controlled fixture verifying the spec's "Supported Render Quantum Sizes" constraint — only powers of 2 in a User-Agent-defined range are accepted), the `AudioContextRenderSizeCategory` enum is exposed globally with values `"default"` and `"hardware"` (controlled fixture verifying the enum's TypeScript-style contract — `AudioContextRenderSizeCategory.Default === "default"` and `AudioContextRenderSizeCategory.Hardware === "hardware"`), `Chrome 152` controls (no configurable render quantum — `renderSizeHint` is `undefined` per the legacy constructor signature) report `new AudioContext({renderSizeHint: 64})` silently ignores the `renderSizeHint` field (Chrome 152 constructs an `AudioContext` with the legacy 128-quantum regardless of the `renderSizeHint` value — controlled fixture verifying the field is silently dropped), `Safari 26.6` controls (Safari WebKit may support `renderSizeHint` per WebKit's own implementation status; controlled fixture verifying `new AudioContext({renderSizeHint: 64}).renderQuantumSize === 64` if supported, undefined if not), `Firefox 155` controls (Firefox does not yet ship `renderSizeHint`; controlled fixture verifying `new AudioContext({renderSizeHint: 64}).renderQuantumSize === 128` — the field is silently ignored on Firefox baseline), the smaller render-quantum (64 frames) reduces `audioContext.baseLatency` (controlled fixture verifying `audioContext.baseLatency < audioContext.baseLatency` for the legacy 128-quantum — the smaller quantum yields lower base latency per the spec's `baseLatency` calculation: `baseLatency = renderQuantumSize / sampleRate`), a render-quantum size of 0 or negative throws `TypeError` (controlled fixture verifying the input-validation contract — `new AudioContext({renderSizeHint: 0})` throws `TypeError` with a message containing "renderSizeHint" or similar), and a controlled fixture verifying the audio-output sample rate (`audioContext.sampleRate`) is unaffected by `renderSizeHint` (changing the render quantum does NOT change the sample rate — they are orthogonal audio-config dimensions; controlled fixture verifying `new AudioContext({renderSizeHint: 64, sampleRate: 48000}).sampleRate === 48000`). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror (Chrome 153 enabled-by-default, "WebAudio: Configurable render quantum" entry), W3C Web Audio API spec at https://webaudio.github.io/web-audio-api/#dom-audiocontextoptions-rendersizehint (the `renderSizeHint` IDL field and the `AudioContextRenderSizeCategory` enum).

### Performance (P)

- [P-189] **[P2] Chrome 153 Stable Rust-Based XML Parser Handles SVG Standalone / External Image Edge Cases Without XSLT So Embedding SVG Assets In Games Doesn't Hit The Legacy Parser** — Chrome 153 stable (Aug 2026, via `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153`, feature 5309598397497344, paired with B-269) ships the SVG-specific edge cases of the Rust XML parser migration: (a) **SVG standalone images** (top-level navigation to `image/svg+xml` documents via `window.location.href = "/game-icon.svg"` or `<a href="/game-icon.svg">` direct-link), and (b) **SVG external images** (embedding an SVG asset as an `<img src="/game-icon.svg">` external-image resource in a main HTML document). These two SVG-specific paths were deferred in v1.219.0 B-269's generic DOMParser / XHR coverage because they have distinct rendering-pipeline integration (SVG-standalone-image is rendered by the navigation / view subsystem, SVG-external-image is rendered by the image-decoding subsystem) and require separate controlled-fixture acceptance. For a controlled fixture serving `/game-icon.svg` over HTTPS with `Content-Type: image/svg+xml` and content `<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="100" height="100"><circle cx="50" cy="50" r="40" fill="red"/></svg>`, assert that `window.location.href = "/game-icon.svg"` followed by `document.documentElement.localName === "svg"` (controlled fixture verifying the top-level SVG navigation is parsed by the Rust parser — the document's root element is the SVG `svg` element per the standalone-image path), the standalone SVG `<circle>` element is queryable via `document.querySelector("circle").getAttribute("fill") === "red"` (controlled fixture verifying the SVG-specific selectors work — Rust parser preserves the SVG element tree correctly), the standalone SVG renders with `document.documentElement.getBoundingClientRect()` returning 100x100 px (controlled fixture verifying the SVG viewport contract — the `width="100" height="100"` attributes are honored), a controlled fixture serving `<html><body><img src="/game-icon.svg" id="svg-img"></body></html>` reports `img.complete === true` after the SVG loads (controlled fixture verifying the external-image path — the SVG is decoded by the image-decoding subsystem and the `<img>` element's `complete` flag is set), the external SVG is queryable via `fetch(img.src).then(r => r.text()).then(t => new DOMParser().parseFromString(t, "image/svg+xml"))` returning a non-null SVG document whose root `.localName === "svg"` (controlled fixture verifying the Rust parser handles the SVG-parse correctly when the SVG arrives via image-decoding pipeline), the Rust parser preserves SVG-namespaced elements (`<svg:foreignObject>` parses with `.namespaceURI === "http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"`; controlled fixture verifying the SVG-namespace contract), the Rust parser rejects malformed SVG with `DOMException` `SyntaxError` (controlled fixture verifying the strictness contract — `<svg><circle></svg>` is rejected because `<circle>` is not closed, unlike legacy parsers that might recover), `Chrome 152` controls (no Rust parser for SVG-image paths; SVG parsing falls back to the legacy parser) report equivalent behavior for the user-visible API surface (controlled fixture verifying the legacy parser handles SVG-image paths correctly — no behavioral regression is introduced by the Rust migration), `Safari 26.6` controls (Safari WebKit's SVG parser handles standalone + external images via libsvg2 + libxml2 — separate codebase) report equivalent SVG-rendering behavior (controlled fixture verifying Safari's existing behavior matches the new Chrome 153 contract for the user-visible API surface), `Firefox 155` controls (Firefox Gecko's SVG parser handles standalone + external images via Gecko's image-decoding subsystem) report equivalent behavior (controlled fixture verifying Firefox's existing behavior matches), the Rust parser handles SVG `<style>` element with embedded CSS (controlled fixture serving `<svg><style>circle { fill: blue; }</style><circle/></svg>` and verifying `document.querySelector("circle").style.fill === "blue"` per the SVG-style-element contract — Rust parser preserves the embedded CSS parsing), the Rust parser handles SVG `<script>` element (controlled fixture serving `<svg><script>window.__svgScriptRan = true;</script></svg>` and verifying the script executes; the SVG parser runs `<script>` elements per the SVG spec), the Rust parser handles SVG `<use>` element with cross-document references (`<use href="/other.svg#element-id"/>`; controlled fixture verifying the cross-document reference contract — Rust parser resolves external-document references correctly), and a controlled fixture verifying the Rust parser handles SVG-as-image with explicit `width`/`height` attributes (`<img src="/icon.svg" width="32" height="32">` and `img.getBoundingClientRect().width === 32` per the SVG-as-image sizing contract). Source: `chromestatuslite.com/?version=153` mirror (Chrome 153 enabled-by-default, "XML parsing in Rust for non XSLT scenarios" entry, sub-bullet "SVG standalone images" and "SVG external images"), Chromium bug https://crbug.com/466303347, W3C XML 1.0 spec at https://www.w3.org/TR/xml, W3C SVG 2 spec at https://www.w3.org/TR/SVG2/.

### Summary — v1.220.0 New Test Cases
| ID | Cat | P | Machine-verifiable focus |
|----|-----|---|--------------------------|
| B-268 | B | P1 | Expose CSSStyleValue hierarchy (CSSStyleValue / CSSKeywordValue / CSSNumericValue / CSSUnitValue / CSSUnparsedValue) to Worker global scopes; Chrome 152 controls report `undefined`; Safari 26.6 / Firefox 155 controls already compliant |
| B-269 | B | P1 | Rust-based XML parser for DOMParser / XHR.responseXML / non-XSLT paths (re-launch from Chrome 151 disabled); XSLT paths remain legacy; `Chrome 152` controls use legacy parser; `Safari 26.6` / `Firefox 155` controls use their own XML parsers |
| W-443 | W | P2 | SpeechRecognitionResult.audioStartTime / .audioEndTime DOMHighResTimeStamp? attributes (Chrome 153 behind `#web-speech-timestamp` flag); `Chrome 152` controls report `undefined` |
| W-444 | W | P2 | Spell Check Custom Dictionary API with `SpellChecker.customDictionary` + `add()` / `remove()` / `has()` / `clear()` (Chrome 153 behind `#spell-check-custom-dictionary` flag); per-document transient; `Chrome 152` controls report `undefined` |
| W-445 | W | P2 | Long Animation Frames API in Dedicated Workers (Chrome 153 behind `#long-animation-frames-in-worker` flag); `PerformanceObserver` `long-animation-frame` entries from inside the worker; `Chrome 152` controls no entries |
| W-446 | W | P2 | JS Self-Profiling API optional `marker` field on `ProfilerSample` (Chrome 153 origin trial); marker enum values `script` / `gc` / `style` / `layout` / `paint` / `other`; OT token required |
| W-447 | W | P2 | WebAudio Configurable Render Quantum — `AudioContextOptions.renderSizeHint` accepts `AudioContextRenderSizeCategory` enum `"default"` / `"hardware"` OR `unsigned long` explicit size (Chrome 153 enabled-by-default, graduating from Edge 152 OT) |
| P-189 | P | P2 | Rust-based XML parser SVG standalone / external image edge cases (paired with B-269); top-level SVG navigation + `<img src="*.svg">` external-image SVG decoding |

*(8 new test cases this cycle. All acceptance criteria are executable through browser automation, WebIDL interface introspection, Worker-context exposure probes, WPT-style controlled fixtures, PerformanceObserver entry introspection, ProfilerSample marker round-trip, AudioContext constructor option round-trip, SVG namespace attribute queries, or XML DOM tree round-trip. No case depends on subjective visual scoring.)*
